Not done: clearance in the listing `WHERE` + keyset pagination, Prometheus/alerts. **Audit remediation (T9)**: the knowledge replay no longer applies the archived envelope blindly: before `apply_event` it anchors `reclassified` to the LIVE `radicados` level (and `created` to `max(event, live)`); with no live radicado it answers `409 stale_event` and the event stays pending (a parked `reclassified(1)` can no longer lower a chunk that went back to level 3). Knowledge 233/48 (281 with `RUN_INTEGRATION=1`), lint green; removing the anchor fails 4 tests.
- `fix(signature,auth,frontend)!`: **the second factor and the credential were checked against the bearer's owner while the signature was attributed to `X-User-Id`; whoever had a TOTP could drop to email on level ≥ 2 acts; the client chose the fingerprint of radicado/expediente chains; one secret served every entity and two uses; and no native signature from `/sign-xml` ever verified** (T8 of the 2026-10-02 audit: AREA-04-05, -02, -09, -11, -12, -06, -10, -13, -08 and AREA-05-02, -07).
  *Why*: the OTP went to the bearer's mailbox, the TOTP was checked against the bearer's device and the personal credential was the bearer's key, while the signature was attributed to `X-User-Id`; the old comment called it "fail-safe" and it was the opposite — reachable from a compromised internal service, not from outside. An actor with an enrolled TOTP could sign level ≥ 2 acts with the email OTP. The client chose `payload_sha256` for radicado/expediente. One `signature_secret` served the signature HMAC and the OTP HMAC for every entity, with a message naming neither entity nor object. A downed credentials channel degraded to native silently with the gate `off`. The HTTP call to auth-service ran under the turn's row lock. `/verify` was documented "public" and any entity user could tell 404 from 200 on reserved acts. Revocation was judged against the current state, not the signature's instant; the timestamp was not cross-checked with the signing certificate or the ESSCertID; `acreditado` was a literal `false` on verification. **Found while testing live**: `/sign-xml` on the native path persisted a different `now()` from the instant inside the HMAC, so no native signature from that path ever verified.
  *What changes*: **signature** — `get_signer_identity` (replaces `get_signer_email`; sends `X-Tenant-Slug` to `/me` and returns `user_id`) compared with the actor BEFORE creating the challenge: `403 identidad_incoherente` + a `firma.identidad_incoherente` entry (also on the TOTP, personal-credential and `/sign-personal` paths). Level ≥ 2 with an active TOTP → `409 totp_requerido` (fail-closed `503`). `radicado`/`expediente` chains ignore the client's `payload_sha256` and `/sign` rejects `payload_xml` where the chain fixes no fingerprint (`422 payload_xml_no_admitido`). HKDF keys per (purpose, entity), a message binding entity+type+object, an OTP HMAC binding `challenge_id`, migration **`020_key_version_hkdf.sql`** (`key_version`, DEFAULT 0 = the previous scheme, which keeps verifying). The personal credential's state is resolved before the transaction; `firma.fallback_nativa_canal_caido` entry and a startup warning with the gate `off`. `/verify` and `GET /firmas/{id}` apply no-read-up inside the `WHERE`; `acreditado` derives from the persisted `nivel_conformidad` and only if the cryptography validates; a revocation AFTER the signature does not invalidate it (`revocacion_posterior`); the timestamp's `genTime` must fall in the signer certificate's validity and the ESSCertID must match (local TSA now emits it; absence is only rejected with `TSA_ESSCERTID_OBLIGATORIO=true`). **auth** — `GET /internal/totp/status`, `user_id` on the valid `/internal/totp/verify` verdict and on `/internal/signing-key/status`; `totp_requerido_por_nivel` removed. **frontend** — the assistant asks for the authenticator when the email challenge answers `totp_requerido`. **docs** — `api.md` ES/EN (with the `payload_sha256` warning, guarded in `shared/tests/`), ADR-016, security audit, roadmap.
  *Verification*: signature-service 480 passed/10 skipped (422/4 before) and 8 passed with `RUN_INTEGRATION=1` (real Postgres: migration `020`, DEFAULT 0/CHECK and the no-read-up gate against real `radicados`/`expedientes`; it caught a real defect); auth-service 287/43; `shared/tests` guards 213 passed; ruff+mypy green; frontend `SignAssistant.test.js` 13 passed. Mutations that fail the tests (restored and rebuilt) cover every item. Live on the local stack (demo tenant, `operador`/`jefe`/`consulta` tokens): a native `/sign-xml` signature verifies (impossible before), as does a `key_version=0` row with the literal previous HMAC; `consulta` (clearance 1) gets a `404` identical to a non-existent UUID for a level-3 radicado's signature; a new XAdES-T verifies; level-2 `challenge`/`sign` with an active TOTP → `409 totp_requerido`; the operator's bearer with `jefe`'s `X-User-Id` → `403 identidad_incoherente`, no challenge, with an audit entry.
  *Impact*: **deploy auth-service BEFORE signature-service** (without `user_id` in its answers signature fails closed); apply migration `020` per tenant with signature-service's runner; rebuild signature-service, auth-service and frontend. **Pending**: enabling `FIRMA_PERSONAL_REQUIRE_XADES=clasificados` (the entity's decision, requires enrolling certificates), an external HSM/KMS, `TSA_ESSCERTID_OBLIGATORIO=true` once the dev corpus is re-stamped, and `X-Internal-Token` on `/internal/totp/verify`.
- `fix(signature,auth,frontend)`: **closing the auditors' findings on T8 (AREA-04-13): a "later" revocation was measured against the server's own clock, and the revocation reason was free text**
  *Why*: `/verify` kept `valida=true` when the key was revoked after the row's `sello_tiempo`, which the server writes and is not a trusted date (RFC 3161); and it did not tell a key compromise (which casts doubt on earlier signatures too, ETSI EN 319 102-1) from a staff departure.
  *What changes*: **auth** — `motivo_revocacion` is a closed vocabulary (RFC 5280: `key_compromise`, `superseded`, `cessation_of_operation`, `affiliation_changed`, `privilege_withdrawn`, `unspecified`): `422` outside it on self- and admin-revoke, tenant migration **`019_motivo_revocacion_vocabulario.sql`** (earlier free text becomes `unspecified`, the original stays in the `signing_key.revocada` audit entry; idempotent CHECK), and `GET /internal/signing-key/revocation-status` also returns `motivo_revocacion`. **signature** — `/verify` takes ONLY a verified TSA's `genTime` as the signature instant: `key_compromise` → `valida=false`, `motivo=clave_comprometida`; no verified TSA → `valida=false`, `motivo=instante_firma_no_cierto`, `revocacion_posterior=null`; with a TSA and a non-compromise reason it keeps `valida=true` + `revocacion_posterior=true`. **frontend** — the reason is a `<select>` with the vocabulary (profile and `/admin/seguridad/claves`); the proxies drop anything outside it.
  *Impact*: deploy auth-service (and apply `019` per tenant) BEFORE signature-service and the frontend; clients sending free text in `motivo` get `422`. *Declared out*: AREA-04-09 (closing the radicado/expediente hash) needs document- and archive-service to expose the radicado PDF and current index SHA-256 over internal HTTP — a three-service increment; meanwhile the `huella_de_contenido=false` audit entry keeps declaring that the identifier is what gets sealed.
- `fix(tenant,infra,auth)!`: **tenant-service connected as the owner (superuser) although the gateway routes to it; creating and suspending institutions left no audit entry; renaming a department with live cases orphaned the inbox; and parameter changes did not record the previous value** (OMI-02, AREA-03-04, AREA-10-02, AREA-10-13, AREA-10-14; batch T7)
  *Why*: migration `008` excused tenant-service as "not routed by the gateway" — false: `/dependencias`, `/catalogos`, `/config`, `/business-days`, `/pinar` reach it, and as owner the append-only guard of `audit_log` did not bind it. Tenant create/patch ran in autocommit with no audit entry; `PATCH /dependencias/{id}` ignored `flow_steps`/`workflow_rules`, which match by NAME; `PUT /config/params/{key}` recorded only the new value.
  *What changes*: tenant-service uses the application role (dev and prod; the owner DSN is only for the migration runner). `POST /tenants` no longer runs DDL: it registers the row as `provisioning_status='pending'` and `scripts/init_tenant.py` (owner) creates the schema and activates it. Create/`PATCH /tenants/{id}` run in one transaction with a `tenant.created`/`tenant.updated` audit entry (before/after, fail-closed). `PATCH /dependencias/{id}` returns `409 dependencia_con_tramite_en_curso` when renaming/deactivating with live steps or rules. `PUT /config/params/{key}` records `antes`/`despues`. New guard test derives gateway-routed services from `proxy.py` and requires the application role in both compose files.
  *Impact*: rebuild tenant-service; production now requires `DB_APP_USER`/`DB_APP_PASSWORD` for it. Institutions are provisioned only via `scripts/init_tenant.py`.

  *Audit closure (T7)*: `DELETE /dependencias/{id}` now applies the same `409 dependencia_con_tramite_en_curso` as `PATCH` (row locked `FOR UPDATE` and count in the same transaction), and the `dependencia.actualizada`/`dependencia.eliminada` audit entries store the previous name and state (`antes`/`despues`). Name-based correlation (instead of code/id) remains declared design debt needing an ADR.
- `fix(archive,document,mcp,frontend)!`: **a single person could propose, endorse and execute the destruction of a batch; linking a radicado sealed into the signed index whatever the linker typed; and destruction could run without the record's lock** (T6 of the 2026-10-02 audit: AREA-06-01, 06-01-B, 06-01-C, 06-02, 07-02, 06-08 partial, 02-08, 07-20)
  *What changes*: `avalar`/`ejecutar` require `crud>=4`; separation of duties (`creado_por <> $5` in the transition's `WHERE`; executor ≠ endorser checked before the first expediente is destroyed; 409 `separacion_de_funciones`); publish/endorse/withdraw/mark-executed are one act with their audit entry; the public batch `GET` answers the same 404 for an unknown slug and an unknown batch; the elimination record's WORM schedules its retry (new `run_once_actas_eliminacion_worm` sweep) and `ejecutar` requires it (409 `acta_sin_worm`); linking resolves number, typology, producing dependency, format, size and hash server-side and cross-checks what was declared (404/422 `metadato_no_coincide`/503 fail-closed); physical-archive `performed_by` and index `generado_por` store the `X-User-Id` UUID, not the login; document-service's internal metadata returns `tracking_number` and `doc_class`; MCP and `/admin/eliminacion` mirror level 4. No migrations.
  *Impact*: deploy document-service BEFORE archive-service; grant level 4 on `USUA_PERM_TRD` to the Committee profile (at least two people) in real tenants; with `PRESERVATION_ENABLED=false` the record is never locked and `ejecutar` answers 409. Declared out: AREA-01-13 (loan `prestado_a` as UUID + clearance gate) and the retype-propagation half of AREA-06-08.
  *T6 review follow-up*: (a) linking honors no-read-up: resolution uses the CALLER's clearance and a radicado above it answers like a missing one (404, no `desacuerdos`), so typology/format/producer/hash of a reserved radicado can no longer be enumerated from its UUID; (b) a radicado without `doc_class` is not linked (422 `radicado_sin_tipificar`; the index XSD requires `<tipologia>`); (c) AREA-01-13 partial: `POST /unidades/{id}/prestamos` applies the lender's no-read-up in the `INSERT … SELECT`'s `WHERE` (403 `clearance_insuficiente`, nothing written). Still open: `prestado_a_user_id` UUID + `min_crud=3` (contract change, needs archival-compliance-auditor validation) and propagating a later re-typing (`document.radicado.retyped`).

- `fix(storage,document,notification)`: **the orphan purge could delete an object another operation had just received through deduplication, any `PERM_RADI` could read and reprocess someone else's batch, and registering a draft did not require holding it** (closing the two auditors' findings on the purge/batch/tenure/webhook round)
  *Why*: (1) `upload` deduplicates by SHA-256 and returns the same `file_id`; `purge_orphan` only saw COMMITTED references, so it deleted an object another import/upload/email had just received and not yet committed. (2) `GET /status` returned every item's `subject` —a batch radicado reclassified to reserved afterwards was readable with `PERM_RADI:1`— and any `PERM_RADI` saw/reprocessed other people's batches. (3) `radicar` (irreversible) did not require the tenure that editing does (AGN Agreement 060/2001). (4) reprocessing cleared the failure reason without leaving it in the audit entry, a batch radicado was indistinguishable from a hand-registered one after the 90-day retention, and the automatic resumption attributed the act to the user. (5) a webhook secret over 255 characters came back in clear in pydantic's 422 (`input`).
  *What changes*: **storage** — migration `017` (`files.dedup_hit_at`); `upload`'s dedup branch does `UPDATE ... RETURNING` (takes the row lock, serialises with the purge's `FOR UPDATE`; if the purge wins, the upload stores a new object) and `purge_orphan` returns `referenciado` when the mark exists; `referencias()` fails closed when a listed table is missing (`table(ausente)`); the docstring that said "Object-Lock rejects it" is fixed (the bucket is versioned: a `remove_object` without `versionId` only creates a delete marker; what protects is `preservacion_worm_objeto`); guardian `shared/tests/test_referencias_de_archivo_cubren_todo_file_id.py` DERIVES the `*file_id*` UUID columns from every service's tenant migrations and compares them with `REFERENCIAS_DE_ARCHIVO` (parser positive control and anti-vacuity included). **document** — `GET /batch/documents/{id}/status` and `POST .../reprocesar`: only the batch's creator or `PERM_RADI` level 3, in the `WHERE`, neutral `404 lote_no_encontrado` (in reprocess, BEFORE the lock so there is no `409` oracle); `subject` carries no-read-up in the SQL (`LEFT JOIN radicados` + `CASE`); `lote_reprocesado` keeps `error_message` (<=500) and `intentos` per item; a batch `radicado_created` carries `origen:"lote"`, `job_id`, `item_index`; automatic resumption writes under the system actor, `canal="system"` and `en_nombre_de`; registering a draft requires the same tenure as editing (neutral 404; `aprobar`/`devolver` still do not); the `PATCH` `UPDATE` carries `estado <> 'radicado'` (409 if it was registered in between); an orphan binary going to `agotado` writes `document.import_binario_agotado` in the same transaction. **notification** — a global `RequestValidationError` handler strips `input`/`ctx` from the 422.
  *Frontend contract (batches)*: the `404` of `GET /status` and `POST /reprocesar` is now `{"detail": {"error": "lote_no_encontrado", "detail": "...", "status": 404}}` (the GET's `detail` used to be a string); the GET requires `X-User-Id`; another person's batch returns 404 even if it exists; `items[].subject` may be `null` (radicado above the caller's level) and each item carries `nivel_seguridad` (`int | null`, `null` when there is no radicado or it is out of reach); the rest of the contract is unchanged.
  *Verification*: storage 413 passed/57 skipped + 457 passed/13 skipped with `RUN_INTEGRATION=1` (real Postgres and two connections for the dedup↔purge race); notification 224/16 + 240 passed; document 679/195 + 874 passed; lint (ruff+mypy) green in all three; the new `shared/tests` guardian is green. Mutations that fail tests: removing the dedup mark in `upload` (2), ignoring it in the purge (1), `continue` on a missing table (1), removing the creator predicate (1), removing the `subject` gate (1), removing the pre-lock check (1), not writing `error_message`/`origen`/system actor/`agotado` (1 each), `radicar` without tenure (2), removing `estado <> 'radicado'` (1), removing the 422 handler (1) and removing a column from `REFERENCIAS_DE_ARCHIVO` (the guardian).
  *Impact*: apply storage migration `017` per tenant and **rebuild** storage-service, document-service and notification-service. Whoever reprocessed or looked at other people's batches with `PERM_RADI` now gets `404`; whoever registered other people's drafts gets `404`.

- `fix(document,storage)`: **editing a draft now requires holding the radicado, a failed import now purges its orphan binaries, and radicado batches resume on their own and can be reprocessed without renumbering** (user decisions of 2026-10-02: AREA-01-N2 follow-up, AREA-09-14, AREA-09-15)
  *Why*: (1) any `PERM_RADI` could edit any non-reserved draft in the tenant. (2) binaries uploaded by a rolled-back import were only recorded and stayed in the store forever. (3) a radicado batch ran in an `asyncio.create_task`: restarting the service left it `pending/processing` forever and a failed item could not be retried.
  *What changes*: **(1)** `PATCH /borradores/{id}` requires HOLDING the radicado (ADR-028 tenure replicated in SQL inside the `UPDATE`'s `WHERE`: active step assigned to the actor, or unassigned and in their dependencia; with no active step, whoever held the last completed one; with no step at all, whoever registered the parent; with no parent, the creator); anyone else gets the same neutral `404` and the row is untouched. `aprobar` and `devolver` do NOT require it (review acts; approval is usually someone else's and is bound to the signature circuit). **(2)** storage-service `POST /internal/purgar-huerfanos` (`X-Internal-Token`): deletes only what that import created, with no reference in the tenant (attachments of any radicado, emails, drafts, migrations, WORM objects) and if the store accepts it, row+object+`file.purgado_huerfano` entry in one transaction. document-service queues the binaries in `import_binario_huerfano` (migration 038, atomic with the `…_sin_radicado` entry), tries to purge at once and, if storage fails, a background **sweep** retries (20 attempts → `agotado`); a confirmed purge writes `document.import_binarios_purgados`. **(3)** migration 039 + sweep: interrupted batches resume under a per-batch advisory lock (several replicas), each item is idempotent (`FOR UPDATE` claim; the consecutive number and the radicado share a transaction: no gaps, no double numbering); failed items stay `failed` with `error_message`, `error_tipo` and `intentos`; the batch ends `completado_con_errores`; `GET .../status` adds `errores[]`, `pending_items`, `reprocesable`; new `POST /batch/documents/{job_id}/reprocesar` (same permission as creating, `document.lote_reprocesado` entry); retention of completed batches without errors (90 days). Batch `status` becomes `VARCHAR(40)`.
  *Verification*: document-service unit 676 passed/186 skipped + integration (real Postgres) 186 passed; storage-service below; lint (ruff+mypy) green in both. Mutations that fail tests: removing the tenure gate, each predicate branch (last step, `completed`, active URD, creator), not passing the actor; removing the reference check, the «pre-existing» check, each reference source, the transaction; treating `error` as terminal, not queueing, not purging after rollback, not retrying; removing the item claim, the lock, the recount, the `completado_con_errores` state, the reprocess filter, the retention, the resumption and the endpoint permission.
  *Impact*: apply migrations `038` and `039` per tenant and **rebuild** document-service and storage-service. Whoever edited other people's drafts with `PERM_RADI` now gets `404`. A batch that used to end `failed` because of failed items now ends `completado_con_errores` (the UI must handle it; the contract is in `docs/en/api.md`). *Declared out*: the race of two concurrent imports of the SAME binary where one fails and purges before the other commits (the reference check and `subido_desde` narrow it, they do not close it); the frontend batch screen does not yet show errors or the «Process the missing ones» button.
  *Frontend*: `/admin/interoperabilidad` ("Carga masiva de documentos" tab) shows "Terminó con errores" with the count of unregistered documents and a table of the failed ones (subject, reason in Spanish, technical text as secondary detail, attempts); when the backend says `reprocesable` and the user holds `PERM_RADI`, it offers "Procesar los faltantes" (process the remaining ones) with a confirmation stating how many will be processed and that already-registered documents keep their number, calls `POST .../reprocesar` through an allowlisted SSR proxy (D-05), polls again until it finishes and maps the `404`/`409` errors (`lote_no_encontrado`, `lote_en_proceso`, `nada_que_reprocesar`) to Spanish messages. Tests: proxy, utilities and screen; the mutation removing the `PERM_RADI` check fails a test.
- `fix(notification,auth,shared,infra)!`: **the webhook HMAC secret was stored in clear: anyone reading the database, a backup or an injection could forge deliveries the receiver would accept; it is now encrypted at rest with a versioned KEK** (debt declared by the T5 closure of the 2026-10-02 audit)
  *Why*: `webhook_subscription.secret` was clear `TEXT`. A hash would not work: the service needs the original secret to sign each delivery (HMAC-SHA256).
  *What changes*: the same cryptographic scheme as auth-service's personal signing key custody, now in `orpycamcp_common.kek` (AES-256-GCM, AAD `tenant:{slug}:webhook:{id}:secret`, fresh nonce per encryption, versioned keyring v1 + `WEBHOOK_SECRET_KEKS`); auth-service delegates to it without changing a byte of what is already stored. Tenant migration `009` (`secret_cifrado`, `secret_nonce`, `secret_kek_version`, three CHECKs; `chk_webhook_secret_sin_claro` forbids writing a new clear secret). Creating a webhook encrypts (the `id` is minted by the application because it is part of the AAD; with no usable KEK it answers `503 webhook_secret_kek_unavailable`); delivery decrypts in memory only to sign and **fails closed**: a row still in clear, or a ciphertext that does not decrypt, is neither delivered nor signed. No GET/list returns the secret, and neither logs nor audit entries carry it. `WEBHOOK_SECRET_KEK` is mandatory (the service does not start without it; in production, `${…:?}`). Operations: `python -m app.ops.cifrar_webhook_secrets` (encrypts legacy rows, sets `secret` to NULL in the same UPDATE and writes the `notification.webhook.secret_cifrado` entry from the application; idempotent) and `python -m app.ops.rewrap_webhook_secrets` (KEK rotation, `--verify` per version).
  *Verification*: notification-service 223 passed/16 skipped (+ 16 integration against real Postgres); auth-service 280 passed/42 skipped + 46 integration; `shared/tests` 191 passed (the 3 reds belong to other batches); lint green on both. Mutations that fail tests: dropping the AAD (5), identity "encryption" (8), writing the clear value in the INSERT (5), returning the secret in GET (2), delivering clear rows (2), not nulling `secret` on encryption (1), AAD without the webhook id (2).
  *Impact*: **define `WEBHOOK_SECRET_KEK`** (`openssl rand -base64 32`) before rebuilding notification-service; apply migrations and run `cifrar_webhook_secrets` BEFORE `up -d` (see `docs/es/deployment.md`). Back the KEK up with the same discipline as the personal signing KEK: if it is lost, every webhook with a secret must be registered again. *Declared out of scope*: the clear `secret` column still exists (always NULL); dropping it is a later migration, once no deployment keeps unencrypted rows.

- `fix(document)`: **corregir `dest_dept` por PATCH dejaba el código de otra dependencia, y el radicado aparecía en `?ubicacion_dept_code=` donde ya no estaba** (ADR-029, auditoría)
  *Por qué*: el PATCH cambiaba el nombre y no el código; la ubicación generada mezclaba ambos. Ahora el código se resuelve por nombre en el mismo UPDATE (único activo, si no `NULL`) y el PATCH de `dest_dept` sobre un radicado `closed` responde 409 `dest_dept_immutable`.

- `fix(notification,workflow,frontend)!`: **a webhook leaked the reservation cause and legal basis, the route sheet of an already-purged reserved radicado became public, and the review chain ignored the radicado's level** (batch T5 of the 2026-10-02 audit: OMI-01, OMI-03, AREA-10-11, AREA-01-N1, AREA-01-06, AREA-10-03, AREA-01-07, AREA-02-06)
  The reclassification event was sent whole because the trimming looked for `nivel_seguridad` while the producer sends `nivel_nuevo`/`nivel_anterior`; workflow events now declare the level and an untyped `document.*`/`workflow.*` event goes out minimal. Webhook create/delete need level 3 and write an audit entry with the actor; delivery pins the validated IP (DNS-rebinding TOCTOU closed). The review chain (`GET`, `decidir`) enforces no-read-up in the query; orphaned `flow_steps`/`flow_events` default to level 3. See `docs/es/changelog.md` for the full entry. Rebuild notification-service, workflow-service and frontend.
- `fix(document,mcp)!`: **a failed radicado burned a number with no audit entry, `import` could take a whole year's registration down with another entity's number, and dispatching a shipment or marking it "delivered" left no audit entry and only needed read level** (T4 of the 2026-10-02 audit: AREA-09-01, AREA-03-08 item 4, AREA-09-14, AREA-09-11, AREA-09-13, AREA-09-03, AREA-09-09, AREA-09-15 partial)
- `fix(document)`: **revisión de T4: un cliente que goteaba el cuerpo de `POST /import` retenía el cupo de todos los tenants, un número propio en 999999 dejaba el siguiente radicado nativo en 7 dígitos, y los binarios de un import revertido quedaban sin asiento** (cuerpo con plazo de 300 s → `408`; techo propio 999998 → `422`; asiento `document.import_binarios_sin_radicado`; purga de huérfanos queda como deuda)
  *Why*: the sequence was taken in autocommit before the radicado transaction (a non-existent `tercero_id` rolled back the radicado and its entry but not the number); `import` advanced `tracking_sequences` from the package number without checking the entity or bounding it (`2026-OTRA-E-2147483647` left `last_seq` at INT_MAX and broke native registration for the year); manual shipment transitions and dispatch wrote no audit entry, needed only read level and the `UPDATE` did not compare the previous state (last writer overwrote a terminal state); import/export built the whole ZIP in memory and hashed on the event loop; the batch API bypassed the metadata template; the stub waybill came from the radicado so two shipments of the same radicado collided with 409.
  *What changes*: the sequence is taken **inside** `conn.transaction()`. `import` reconciles the counter only for this entity's code and rejects with `422` sequences above 999999 and years outside [1900, current]. Shipments: the dispatch and manual-status POSTs require `PERM_RADI_SALIDA` level 3, write `document.envio_registrado` / `document.envio_estado` (actor = `X-User-Id`) in the same transaction, and the `UPDATE` carries previous state and clearance in its `WHERE` (0 rows becomes `409 transicion_invalida`; the operator callback uses the same guard). Waybills derive from a per-shipment uuid4. `import`/`export` require `USUA_PERM_EXPEDIENTE` level 3, run validation/hashing/ZIP building in threads, take a per-process slot (busy becomes `429 operacion_ocupada` with `Retry-After`), cut the body read at 512 MiB and cap export attachments at 512 MiB. The batch validates the active template of the `doc_class` and stores `metadata_template_id`, and numbers with the registration-timezone year. MCP catalog: both shipment write tools declare level 3. No migration.
  *Verification*: document-service unit 620 plus integration against real Postgres 150 (770 total), mcp-server 236, `shared/tests` level guardian green, lint clean; every mutation (drop each fix) kills tests.
  *Impact*: rebuild document-service and mcp-server. Holders at level 1-2 now get `403`; the `/envios` and interoperability screens do not mirror the level yet. *Declared out*: deleting orphan blobs of a rolled-back import (storage deduplicates by SHA-256), resuming/purging `batch_jobs`, async job/streaming for import-export, a dedicated dispatch permission, the gateway `/auth/validate` cache MAC and the audit-doc fix (T2/T14).

- `fix(document,mcp)!`: **a level-1 `PERM_RADI` could read and edit drafts answering reserved radicados, a reserved subject reached the assistant's provider through two listings, and the declarative "signature" stored a client-chosen hash** (batch T3 of the 2026-10-02 audit: AREA-11-X1, AREA-11-X2, AREA-01-N2, AREA-05-03-S)
  *Why*: `GET /terceros/{id}/radicados` returned the `subject` of RESERVED radicados with no clearance gate, and the assistant's residency barrier only strips what declares `nivel_seguridad`, which the schema lacked. `RespuestaItem` had the same flaw. `borrador` had no level at all and `editar` wrote no audit entry. `POST /documents/{id}/signatures` stored the body hash, at read level, with no `audit_log` entry.
  *What changes*: the clearance gate goes in the same `WHERE` (with the merged-records `OR` parenthesised), items declare `nivel_seguridad`, and the read is audited. Migration `035_borrador_nivel_seguridad.sql`: drafts inherit the parent's level; get/list/update/set_estado filter on the effective level (`GREATEST(snapshot, parent's current level)`), neutral 404; `PATCH` writes an atomic `borrador.editado` entry. Declarative signature: `PERM_FIRMA` level 2, mandatory `anexo_id`, server-resolved hash from storage (body `content_hash` ignored, 503 if storage is down), `document.signature_created` entry in the same transaction. New guardian `shared/tests/test_contenido_declara_su_nivel.py` (frozen exception list that can only shrink).
  *Verification*: document-service unit 576 plus integration against real Postgres, mcp-server 236, `shared/tests` 182, lint clean; every mutation (drop each gate, drop the parentheses, drop `GREATEST`, drop the audit entries, use the body hash, move the audit out of the transaction, drop level 2, drop `nivel_seguridad` from the schemas) kills tests.
  *Impact*: apply migration `035` per tenant and **rebuild** document-service and mcp-server. *Declared out, on purpose*: whether to retire the signature POST with `410` (user decision); whether editing a draft requires being its creator; `SearchHit`/`RadicadoMetaItem` still lack `nivel_seguridad`.
  *Audit remediation (T3)*: **(High)** `radicar` created the registry entry at level 1 even when the draft answered a reserved one: it now inherits the CURRENT parent level (`GREATEST` with the snapshot), its basis/cause/term (a parent without basis is motivated by inheritance: never falls to PUBLIC) and the `responde_a` link. **(Medium)** migration `036_signatures_origen.sql`: legacy declarative signatures become `origen='cliente_no_verificado'` and `verify` excludes them; new ones carry `origen='servidor'`. **(Medium)** `aprobar` (user and circuit-completed internal route) and `devolver` record `borrador.aprobado` / `borrador.devuelto` in the same transaction as the state change. *Verification*: document-service 744 (`RUN_INTEGRATION=1`), lint clean; mutations that break tests: dropping level inheritance, dropping the `origen` filter, renaming the audit action. *Impact*: apply `036` per tenant and rebuild document-service.

- `fix(workflow)`: **closing an overdue procedure recorded the deadline as "unknown"** (E05, ADR-028)
  *Why*: `fuera_de_plazo` in the `workflow.finalizar` audit entry only looked at `flow_steps.due_at`, which is almost never set. Now the step deadline wins if present, otherwise the radicado's legal due date (`radicados.due_date`); `null` only when neither exists.
  *Verification*: workflow-service 397 with `RUN_INTEGRATION=1`; new test with positive and negative control, killed by the mutation that ignores the radicado due date.

> **Coverage note (2026-08-29).** This English changelog carries the most recent entries and the
> project's milestones, **not every entry**: the Spanish page (`docs/es/changelog.md`) has 410
> entries against 241 here. The gap is historical —older increments that were never translated—
> and it is stated here rather than left for the reader to discover: a changelog that looks
> complete and is not is worse than one that declares its scope. New entries are written in both
> from now on.

- `feat(workflow,document,mcp)`: **the workflow moves the radicado's status — distributing no longer leaves the radicado in `registered`** (E05, E01, ADR-028)
  *Why*: radicado status and step progress were two machines that did not talk to each other (domain decision left open on 2026-10-01). Distributing, transferring and processing advanced `flow_steps`; `radicados.status` only changed if someone manually pressed "Distribute"/"In progress" —a `PATCH` with `status`— so the dashboard, inbox and search filtered on states the workflow never wrote. In Orfeo distributing *is* a status change. *How*: workflow-service writes `radicados.status` **in the same transaction as the step**, after mutating it and before the audit entry (if `audit.append` fails neither step nor status remains), through **one pure function** (`estado_radicado.py::derivar_estado_radicado`) whose literal twin is the backfill's `CASE`: active step `pending` → `distributed`, `in_progress` → `in_progress`. Assign, transfer, return, roll back, cascade reassign and the automatic distribution on registration all apply it; completing without marking as read leaves `distributed` (declared). **`in_progress` only via the holder's `marcar_leido`**, which required closing a pre-existing gap: `execute_transaction` did not check holdership, so anyone with `PERM_TRAMITAR` could mark someone else's file as read (`transaction_types.requires_holder`, migration 012; non-holder → the same `404 no_active_step`, no oracle). **The only close is `POST /workflows/{id}/finalizar`** (`PERM_TRAMITAR`, holder only, mandatory `comentario`, `tipo_tx=finalizar`): not a `transaction_type`, because that catalogue is tenant-configurable and an irreversible act cannot live there. `closed` and `anulado` are **terminal**: any act that moves the step answers `409 radicado_terminal` and rolls back; `informar`/`agendar` stay allowed. The cascade puts steps back to `pending` (Orfeo `LEIDO=0`), **excludes terminals** (`skipped_terminal`), takes locks in order and writes `workflow.estado_radicado` per changed radicado. After commit `workflow.radicado.estado_cambiado` is published (best-effort). document-service: the `PATCH` **rejects `status`** with `422 status_managed_by_workflow` (dropping the field was not enough: Pydantic would have ignored the extra and the client would believe it changed). MCP: `actualizar_radicado` loses `status`; new `finalizar_tramite` (irreversible) → **207 tools, 75 writes, 27 irreversible**. Backfill `013_radicado_estado_desde_flujo.sql` with an evidence table `radicado_estado_recalculo`, no `audit_log` writes from SQL, idempotent.
  *Verification*: workflow-service **308 unit tests (18 integration skipped) and 18 integration tests against real Postgres** (`RUN_INTEGRATION=1`, new `tests/integration/` tier: atomicity with a forced `audit.append` failure, terminals leaving every count unchanged, cascade, backfill run twice —the 2nd, 0 rows— and every row compared with the Python function); mcp-server 228; `shared/tests` 171; `lint.sh` clean on the three services. **Verified by mutation (7, each one fails tests)**: removing the sync from `_create_step`, moving it out of the transaction, removing the terminal guard, removing the holder check, inverting the backfill `CASE`, not excluding terminals in the cascade and ignoring `finalizar`'s flag.
  *Frontend*: `/bandeja` drops the manual «Distribuir»/«Iniciar trámite» buttons (and their `status` `PATCH`, which now returns `422 status_managed_by_workflow`). The detail drawer offers **«Marcar en trámite»** (`marcar_leido`), **«Finalizar trámite»** —mandatory 5-500 character comment plus a confirmation checkbox, new proxy `POST /bandeja/api/{id}/finalizar`— and **«Devolver»** only to the **holder** of the active step (`assigned_to` = me, or unassigned and in my dependencia, read from `GET /current`), with `PERM_TRAMITAR`; the backend re-validates. Spanish messages for `409 radicado_terminal`, `404 no_active_step` and `422 status_managed_by_workflow`. *After the audit*: «Finalizar» additionally requires **`PERM_TRAMITAR` level ≥3** (mirrors `min_crud=3`, read from `permission_levels`; not offered at level 2) and asks for a mandatory **closing reason** (selector: Respondido · No requiere respuesta (NRR) · Traslado por competencia · Otro) that the proxy validates (`422` without calling the gateway) and forwards with the comment (`{comentario, motivo_cierre}`, allowlist). With no active step it is also offered to whoever held the **last completed step** of a non-terminal radicado (reads `GET /history`). The Board (`WorkflowBoard`/`WorkflowCard`) honours **holdership**: «En trámite», Trasladar, Trámite, Completar, Revertir and dragging to «En trámite» only for the holder; a colleague sees the card but cannot act on it. New messages for `404 step_not_found` and `404 radicado_not_found`. After R15–R21: «Asignar a flujo» is offered only with no active step and to whoever held the **last completed step** or, with no steps at all, whoever registered the radicado (`registered_by` from the detail compared with the internal `user_id`, not the `sub`); with a null `registered_by` (imported/ingest) or an unloaded history it is **not offered** (fail-closed; the backend answers `404 no_active_step`). «Devolver» and «Revertir» follow the `_holder_condition` holdership (including a department's ownerless step; `403 not_current_holder` no longer exists) and «Revertir» only while the step is `pending`. Spanish message for `404 no_flow` (missing flow or radicado above the access level).
  *Declared out of scope, on purpose*: outgoing and internal radicados have no outward distribution; annulment did not cancel the active step (**fixed in the remediation**, below); `rollback` only from `pending`; completing without marking as read leaves `distributed`; `finalizar`/`devolver` on a closed radicado give the neutral 404 (a closed one has no step, and saying "it is closed" to someone who holds nothing would be an oracle); the INT-05 import keeps the package's `status`. **Pending**: the frontend ("Finish processing" button, removing manual "Distribute"/"In progress", translating the 409).
  *Impact*: apply migrations (`012`, `013` per tenant) and **rebuild** workflow-service, document-service and mcp-server. Any client sending `status` to `PATCH /documents/{id}` now gets `422`. `finalizar` moves to level 3 (see remediation). With the remediation, also: apply `014`/`015` per tenant and document-service `034`, and **rebuild** document-service and mcp-server as well; a development tenant that already applied 013 before the remediation must be recreated (013 had not been released and was edited instead of adding a corrective one).
  *Audit remediation (2026-10-02, same ADR-028)*: after the security and archival-compliance reviews the backend was corrected: **(1)** `assign`/`transfer` are no longer the side door to closing —B could self-assign someone else's radicado (even a reserved one) and "Finalizar" then closed it—: inside the transaction and after the lock, the **actor's clearance goes in the `WHERE`** of the radicado read (`404 radicado_not_found`, same as a nonexistent radicado) and, with an active step, **only its holder** may distribute or transfer it (`404 no_active_step`); automatic distribution on registration is a system actor (`sistema=True`, only `auto_distribute`); forcibly redistributing someone else's step stays in the admin cascade. **(2)** The `409 radicado_terminal` oracle is closed (the holder gate runs before synchronising; `anular` becomes `requires_holder`, migration 014, and any transaction that moves the step requires holding it). **(3)** `finalizar` requires `PERM_TRAMITAR` **level 3** (was 1, the read level); it leaves `SIN_NIVEL_DECLARADAS` and the MCP catalog mirrors it. **(4)** `complete_step` uses **the same holder definition** (before, `assigned_to IS NULL` was enough to close another dependencia's ownerless step, and a closed step could be "re-closed"). **(5)** MCP `finalizar_tramite` is confirmed by **typing the radicado** (`confirmar_con`), not `CONFIRMO`. **(6)** The cascade takes **one advisory scope lock** (shared in every act, exclusive in the cascade) plus ordered row locks, instead of one per radicado, which exhausted `max_locks_per_transaction`. **(7)** **Annulment cancels the active step in the same transaction** (document-service, `flow_events.tipo_tx='anulacion'`); migration `015_sanear_pasos_zombi.sql` cleans up existing ones with an insert-only evidence table (`flow_step_saneamiento`), and `inbox`/`pending`/overdue exclude terminal radicados. **(8)** INT-05 import rejects packages carrying `anulado` (closed enum in `RADICADO_SCHEMA`), records the `status_origen` in the audit entry, and migration `034_radicado_status_check.sql` (document-service) closes the domain of `radicados.status` with a `CHECK` (`NOT VALID` + validation if the data allows). **(9)** The 013 backfill leaves **insert-only** evidence (`tracking_number`, `paso_activo`, `origen`), no longer touches `updated_at`, and the migration *runner* writes the `workflow.estado_radicado.backfill` anchor entry (count + SHA-256) to `audit_log`, once per tenant. **(10-12)** `finalizar` requires `motivo_cierre` (`respondido|nrr|traslado_competencia|otro`), its audit entry adds `step_id`, `flow_event_id`, `tiene_respuesta`, `fuera_de_plazo` and `en_expediente`, and it is allowed without an active step to whoever held the last completed step. **(13)** `_cambios` is emptied at the start of every act (the `xfail` test turned green). **Pre-existing defect found while testing against real Postgres**: `AnulacionRepository.decide` conditioned on `estado = 'pendiente'`, a value the `CHECK` never admits, so **no annulment approval could ever be applied** (always `409 anulacion_ya_decidida`); mocks hid it. Fixed to `'solicitada'`.

  *Security re-audit (2026-10-02, same ADR-028; R15-R21 and RN-8)*: **(A)** `finalizar`, `complete_step`, `marcar_leido`, `rollback`, `devolver` and every holder-gated transaction **check the holder's clearance** (level in the `WHERE`; a level-1 member of the dependencia can no longer close a reserved radicado distributed to it with no owner); same neutral 404. **(B)** A radicado **with no active step** is no longer takeable by anyone with `PERM_TRAMITAR`: with history, whoever held the last step distributes it; with no step at all, whoever registered it (`registered_by`); a closed one gives `404 no_active_step` on distribution (was `409`). **(C/H)** Transactions without holder-gating (vobo, informar, nrr, agendar, solicitar_firma…) apply clearance and return `404 no_flow`, the same as a nonexistent radicado or one with no steps: they no longer leak a reserved radicado's `tracking_number`/`to_dept` nor tell "exists" from "does not exist". **(D/E)** `rollback`/`devolver` read the step and the holder check inside the transaction after the lock and `apply_*` repeat the condition; both use the same holder definition (a dependencia member can now revert/return an ownerless step). **(F)** MCP `finalizar_tramite` declares `motivo_cierre` (mandatory, enum): every call used to give 422. **(G)** The race between two annulment decisions gives `409 anulacion_ya_decidida`, not 500. **RN-8** the `auto_distribute` guard is repeated inside the lock. Declared, not fixed: annulment may annul a `closed` radicado; `USUA_PERM_ADMIN` at `min_crud=1` for the cascade; `TRUNCATE` not covered by the evidence triggers.
  *Remediation verification*: workflow-service **376 tests with `RUN_INTEGRATION=1`** (322 unit + 54 integration, 0 `xfail`), document-service **695 with integration** (569 unit), mcp-server **230**, `shared/tests` **171**, `lint.sh` clean on all three. Mutations (each fails tests): remove `assign`'s holder gate, remove the clearance from the `WHERE`, `finalizar` back to level 1 (the `shared` guard also fails), `complete_step` with the old definition, cascade with a lock per radicado, remove step cancellation from annulment, remove the enum from the import schema, remove the `CHECK`, and revert to `'pendiente'`.
  *Contracts that change for the frontend*: `POST /workflows/{id}/finalizar` requires `motivo_cierre` (`respondido|nrr|traslado_competencia|otro`) besides `comentario`, and `PERM_TRAMITAR` level 3 (`403` below it); `assign`/`transfer` answer `404 no_active_step` to a non-holder of the active step and `404 radicado_not_found` if the radicado is out of reach; `PATCH /{step_id}/complete` answers `404 step_not_found` for someone else's step **or an already closed one**; `POST /{id}/transactions` with `anular`/`marcar_leido` (or any type that moves the step) answers `404 no_active_step` to a non-holder, also on terminal radicados (no longer `409`); `inbox`/`pending` no longer list steps of terminal radicados.
  *Declared out of this batch*: the "reopen" act (MoReq: controlled reopening with admin permission); outgoing/internal radicados without distribution; completing an `in_progress` step leaves the radicado `in_progress` with no active step (kept); a radicado with history and no active step may be distributed by anyone with `PERM_TRAMITAR` and clearance (R1 residual).

- `fix(frontend,mcp)`: **the dashboard told someone with 101 pending steps they were "all caught up", alerts hid 127 overdue deadlines, and the assistant could not be opened** (E22, E18, RF-RAD-04, RF-UI-06) — Three defects **no suite could see**, found by walking the app in a browser against the running stack. *1.* The dashboard's "Pending" and "Alerts" modules filtered radicados in `distributed`/`in_progress`, but the workflow advances the **steps**, not the radicado, whose status only changes through manual buttons in the inbox List view: all 208 radicados were `registered`, the board had 101 pending steps, and 127 response deadlines were past with "no upcoming deadlines" on screen. Pending items now come from `GET /workflows/inbox` — the same query as the `/bandeja` board — and alerts include `registered`, since a response term runs from registration. The tests had enshrined the defect by building radicados in `distributed`; mutation-verified (8 fail with the old filter). *2.* A scaffold-era global rule `.assistant-dock { transform: translateX(100%) }` pushed the panel off-screen (`x=1901` in a 1916 viewport) while the backdrop dimmed the page, and the e2e test passed because Playwright's `toBeVisible()` accepts an element outside the viewport. It now asserts `toBeInViewport`, and a guard test forbids global sheets from styling the panel's root class. *3.* With no model configured the panel promised the feature "will be enabled soon"; the backend now says **which** cause applies (no model configured vs. provider down), the proxy forwards it, and the panel shows it. **Left open, as a domain decision rather than a fix**: radicado status and workflow progress are two machines that do not talk to each other.

- `docs(api,architecture,roadmap)`: **the tool catalogue grew fourfold and the reference stayed on the old snapshot** (E18, ADR-019, ADR-020) — *Why*: both API references declared "45 tools, 16 writes" and a coverage of "367 operations, 41 covered". The real catalogue is **206 tools, 74 writes**, covering **203 of the 342 exposed operations (59 %)**. The figure was not lost for lack of recording — the changelog logged every increment: 106, 112, 146, 157, 170, 201 — it was **a hand-written number sitting next to one that moves**, and it stayed that way for eight months. Three statements went with it from incomplete to **false**: that the assistant is "read-only" and excludes `radicar_documento` (it now offers all 206, and no write is dispatched: a signed proposal is returned for the person to confirm), that the acts left out are "annulling, transferring, repealing TRD/TVD…" (all of them exist today as tools), and the English roadmap's "Deferred: enabling write tools behind double confirmation", closed months ago. *How*: the 206-row table is **generated from `services/mcp-server/app/tools/catalog.py`**, grouped by epic, carrying the permission and CRUD level the catalogue itself declares, and marking the 26 irreversible acts and the 5 that require typing a value off the card. The exclusion list is replaced by the **15 acts the server publishes** in `mi_alcance`, with the four criteria that separate them. In the English page the operation column **is not translated, and the reason is stated**: it is the label the server returns in the confirmation card, not prose.

- `docs(architecture,getting-started)`: **the twelfth service was missing from the map** (ADR-020) — *Why*: `whisper-service` has a folder, a Dockerfile, port 8012 and a service in `docker-compose.yml`, and appeared in neither `architecture.md` nor `api.md` nor `CLAUDE.md`, which still said "11 microservices". Only the changelog, the roadmap and ADR-020 named it — **the pages that narrate, not the ones that describe the system**. *How*: added to the port table and the diagram in both languages, with a section of its own covering its three deliberate traits (off by default under the `assistant` profile and with no transcription `stub`; no gateway route and no published port; the only service with no database and no per-tenant schema), plus `docker compose --profile assistant` in getting started.

- `docs(api)`: **two endpoints with no entry** — `POST /signature/internal/circuitos/{tipo}/{id}/invalidar`, which voids the live circuits of a draft **whose text changed** (best-effort and idempotent: the hard guarantee is the digest comparison at registration; this is what stops someone being left with a tray turn for a document that no longer exists), and `GET /api/v1/catalogos`, which says which catalogues exist. *Verification, without Docker*: mechanical code↔docs cross-check on four axes — **397 routes** (the 27 residuals checked by hand as grouped notation), **206/206 tools** present in ES and EN, **51/51 screens**, **27/27 ADRs**. *And a trap of my own*: the first extractor accused **95 endpoints** of being undocumented; every one of them was there. The instrument gets checked before the subject.

- `feat(storage,infra)!`: **MinIO shut down its open-source project; the object store moves to RustFS, and the engine was chosen by tests** (E10, E12, ADR-027, AGN Agreement 001/2024 art. 4.3.2.4 P1) — *Why*: MinIO **archived its open-source project** and pulled its images from every public registry. Verified with a positive control (`alpine`, `node` and `keycloak` downloaded fine at the same moment, so it was neither the network nor rate limiting): quay.io answers `denied` and lists **zero** tags, Docker Hub 404s, the `dl.min.io` binary returns **410 Gone**, and even the **digest** recorded in the compose file no longer resolves. Without a local cache the whole stack stopped booting. *The lesson outlives the incident*: a pin protects you from having the version changed, **not from having it taken away** — a public registry is a distribution channel, not an archive. — *How the replacement was chosen*: not from a datasheet. A **WORM conformance battery against a real S3 store** (`test_worm_object_lock_real_s3.py`) with seven tests covering what the code actually uses (COMPLIANCE, legal hold, bucket Object Lock, versioning, `copy_object` inheriting the lock, re-sealing a locked key), each with a positive and a negative control. Measured: **RustFS 1.0.0 → 7/7**, **MinIO (baseline) → 7/7**, **Garage → 0/7**. The decisive test uses the **root** credential, because a delete that fails *against root* is what separates COMPLIANCE from GOVERNANCE — and under GOVERNANCE the credential that could lift the retention is the very one the writing service holds. — *Rejected*: **SeaweedFS** accepted `PutObjectRetention` in COMPLIANCE with a 200 **and still allowed the delete**, and its fixes are merged but **unreleased**; **Garage** has no Object Lock and structurally cannot; **Ceph RGW** stops being a Compose service; **building MinIO from AGPL source** was the recommended bridge while there was data to migrate, dropped once the whole corpus proved to be test data. — *Collateral damage repaired, unnoticed until now*: `infra/backup.sh` and `infra/restore.sh` invoked the `minio/mc` image, **also removed** — backup and restore were broken, including `mc retention set`, which is what reinstates WORM retention on restore. Migrated to `aws-cli`. **And an improvement that came with it**: `mc retention set` only took durations in days, so restoring a retention rounded it **up** and moved the expiry date on every restore; `put-object-retention` takes `RetainUntilDate`, so the term is again the one the institution set once. — *Impact*: the stack boots and rebuilds from scratch again; WORM conformance goes from a reputation to an **executable proof**, and stays as the adoption condition for any future engine.

- `fix(storage)`: **the system ordered WORM retention and never looked at it again** (E10, ADR-023, ADR-027) — *Why*: `set_object_retention` appeared in three files and `get_object_retention` in **none**. After writing the retention the service recorded `retention_mode='COMPLIANCE'` and a PREMIS `FIJACION` event as `ok` — it **attested** a protection it had never verified, on the store's word. With a store that accepts and does not enforce, the transfer record — signed, with its digest — **asserts** a guarantee that does not exist: a cryptographically impeccable document that is **false in its content**, which is worse than having no WORM at all. With a perverse twist: the disposition gate blocks **legitimate** deletions on the strength of that row, while nothing stops an illegitimate deletion in the store — the control works backwards. — *How*: `verificar_retencion_aplicada` re-reads the retention just written and aborts if the mode differs, if the date is **earlier** than requested, or if the store cannot answer at all; invoked **fail-closed** after protecting the index or the transfer record, after sealing the AIP (before replicating it, since replicating an object whose protection could not be confirmed would propagate the doubt to the second copy), and after renewing the retention. Same pattern as the pre-existing fixity check: when in doubt, do not seal and do not record.

- `refactor(rebrand)`: full rename `orfeomcp` → **`OrpycaMCP`** / **`orpycamcp`** before v0.01 — *Why*: the project is the evolution of Orfeo under the **Orpyca** concept ("Orfeo Pyme Calidad") with AI connectivity via **MCP** (Model Context Protocol) — *Impact*: by-surface convention — **brand** `OrpycaMCP`; **data/infra token** `orpycamcp` (DB `orpycamcp_db`, Keycloak realm `orpycamcp`, buckets `orpycamcp-{slug}-*`, Redis streams `orpycamcp.*`, Python package `orpycamcp_common`, network `orpycamcp-net`, containers `orpycamcp-*`); **repo/images/Compose project** `orpyca-mcp`; **company/web/org** `Orpyca`/`orpyca.com`/GitLab `orpyca`. **Requires recreating volumes**; the repo folder is renamed to `orpyca-mcp` (manual step)
- `docs(claude)`: `CLAUDE.md` synced with the real architecture — 11 microservices with correct ports; removed SQLAlchemy/Alembic references (contradicted ADR-003: asyncpg + raw SQL); phases updated per specDrive
- `chore(ci)`: CI unified on **GitLab** — removed `.github/workflows/`; README/badges/clone point to `gitlab.com/orpyca/orpyca-mcp`
- `chore(compose)`: dev published ports moved to a high, distinct range (**19xxx / 15432 / 16379**) to avoid clashes with other dev stacks; internal ports and Docker-network communication unchanged
- `fix(compose)`: `signature-service` and `knowledge-service` now wait for `postgres` *healthy* (`depends_on: condition: service_healthy`) — they previously exited at startup with `ConnectionRefused`


### Deprecated

- `workflow,document,tenant`: the dependencia **name** fields as a key (`to_dept`, `from_dept`, `assign_to_dept`, `?dept=`, `dest_dept` on PATCH/drafts) remain as a deprecated alias for one cycle (`Deprecation` header + `Link`); the canonical field is `*_dept_code` (ADR-032).

### Added

- `feat(document,workflow,mcp)`: **the list, search and detail now say where the radicado is now, not just who it was addressed to** (E05, E01, ADR-029)
  *Why*: `2026-DEMO-E-000117` showed the registration dependencia ("Oficina de Tecnología") after being distributed to Talento Humano and returned to Control Interno. `dest_dept` is a legal snapshot that does not change; where the radicado is could only be rebuilt by walking `flow_steps`. In Orfeo it is `radi_depe_actu`/`radi_usua_actu`: two columns on the radicado itself that every distribution rewrites.
  *What changes*: document-service migration **`037_radicado_ubicacion.sql`** (columns `flujo_dept_code`/`flujo_dept`/`flujo_usuario` + two **generated** columns `ubicacion_dept_code`/`ubicacion_dept`, computed as pairs, indexed); workflow-service `sincronizar_estado_radicado` writes them in the **same `UPDATE` as `status`** (the cascade too), with the new pure function `derivar_ubicacion` (the active step; failing that, **the most recently closed one**, not the highest-numbered; terminal radicados freeze; the code only when the name matches **one** active dependencia) and migration **`016_radicado_ubicacion_desde_flujo.sql`** (backfill, insert-only evidence `radicado_ubicacion_recalculo`, a `workflow.ubicacion_radicado.backfill` anchor written by the runner, `RAISE` if document-service has not migrated yet). `GET /documents` and `GET /search` accept `?ubicacion_dept_code=` (same condition in rows and `COUNT`); responses carry `flujo_*` and `ubicacion_*`; `flujo_usuario` only in the detail. Reports: `by_ubicacion` (no closed or annulled) and the CSV section `dependencia` becomes `dependencia_radicacion` (+ `ubicacion_actual`). INT-01 export carries the location as informational data that import ignores. MCP: `ubicacion_dept_code` on `listar_radicados` and `buscar_radicados`; `dest_dept[_code]` described as "registration". **None of this appears on public surfaces** (verification by code, OAI-PMH, CMIS).
  *Verification*: workflow-service **441 passed** (`RUN_INTEGRATION=1`; 347 + 94 integration skipped without it) including real-Postgres integration reproducing the real case (register in Tecnología → Talento Humano → return to Control Interno ⇒ Control Interno, `dest_dept` untouched), rollback, rollback + complete, finish, annulment freezing, cascade, duplicate name → `NULL` code, atomicity under an audit failure, and a backfill compared row by row with the Python function (2nd run: 0 changes; the trigger rejects `UPDATE`/`DELETE`); document-service **816 passed** (`RUN_INTEGRATION=1`; 658 + 158 skipped without it); mcp-server **243**; lint clean. Mutations (each one fails tests): remove the `flujo_*` write, order "last" by `step_number`, take the filter out of the `COUNT`, add the location to `PublicVerifyResponse`. Applied to the live stack: `2026-DEMO-E-000117` ends in **Control Interno** (code 907).
  *Frontend*: `/bandeja` (list and detail), `/busqueda` (column and `ubicacion_dept_code` filter), `/reportes` (new «Por ubicación actual (en trámite)» panel and the rename to «Por dependencia de radicación»), the main panel («Dónde está el trámite», on `by_ubicacion`) and the expediente radicado picker now say **where the radicado is now**, not who it was addressed to. The detail separates «Ubicación actual» (with a named responsible person, never the UUID; «Cerrado en X» for terminal states) from «Dependencia de radicación». Without `ubicacion_dept` the cell reads «—»: it never falls back to `dest_dept`, which would be inventing the location. The search proxies accept `ubicacion_dept_code` only as a positive integer. New helper `lib/utils/ubicacionRadicado.js`; types in `lib/types/api.js`. 2,237 frontend tests (19 new), `svelte-check` 0/0; each mutated separately and red with its test: the list and search cells going back to `dest_dept`, the «Cerrado en» block, the «Dependencia de radicación» block, the filter removed from each proxy and from the `load`, the reports panel and the dashboard card reading `by_dependencia`, the `dest_dept` fallback in the helper and the expediente picker column. *UX review (2026-10-02)*: with no flow steps the detail now says so («Sin repartir — en X»; an outgoing/internal radicado with no destination reads «en la dependencia productora: X»), and a terminal that never went through the flow reads «Anulado/Cerrado sin pasar por el flujo — en X», instead of «aún en su dependencia de radicación»; «Ubicación actual» is now the first, highlighted row of the detail, ahead of the filing department; the location moves to an «En X» line under the subject in the `/bandeja` and `/busqueda` lists at ≤768 px, where the column is hidden; the `/busqueda` filter moves from a numeric code to a **department-catalog selector** («name — code»; it filters by code, so a radicado with an ambiguous name —no code— does not appear in it); `/bandeja/api/users` (requires `PERM_RADI`) is no longer called without it nor repeated after a failure, and the owner reads «A cargo de un responsable de la dependencia» with no error notice; the main panel says «No disponible» when the summary lacks `by_ubicacion` instead of «Sin radicados en trámite». Frontend tests with one mutation per major point turned red.
  *Impact*: apply `037` (document-service) and **then** `016` (workflow-service) per tenant; **rebuild** document-service, workflow-service and mcp-server. The reports CSV no longer has the `dependencia` section. *Declared out of scope, on purpose*: `flow_steps.to_dept_code`.
- `fix(auth)`: **the audit entry for renaming or deleting an RBAC group now keeps the previous value** (ADR-010, MoReq/ISO 15489). `update_group`/`delete_group` read the row `FOR UPDATE` inside the same transaction and record `{campos, antes, despues}` and `{name, description}`. No migration. auth-service 322 with `RUN_INTEGRATION=1`, lint clean, mutation verified (3 tests fail). Declared out: `min_crud=3` on webhook/template writes and a dedicated audit-read permission (pre-existing; next batch).

- `feat(mcp)`: **archival instruments in the MCP** — TRD/TVD circuit, document types, metadata and PINAR (E18, F9)
  The census's largest block (69 operations, 6% → **55%**). The TRD fixes how long each thing is kept and what happens to it at the end: it is the instrument everything else hangs from.
  **34 new tools**: the full version circuit for TRD and TVD —create · new version · approve · return · validate · derogate · register RUSD—, the history, expired dispositions and their decision, expedientes pending review after a table change, document types, metadata elements and templates, and PINAR reads plus progress logging.
  **Friction goes on the VERSION NUMBER.** Validating and derogating change which table governs classification from then on, and the irreparable error is not validating —that is the point— but validating **the wrong version**: it cannot be corrected by another act; a new version must be opened and the whole circuit walked again, with the wrong table governing meanwhile.
  **And the steps that have a way back ask for nothing.** The circuit offers `devolver` to return from approved to draft: putting the irreversible act's friction there would spend it where a remedy exists.
  **PINAR is read and progress-logged; it is not formulated.** Formulating a PINAR is planning with the Committee present, not a sequence of inserts. Declared in the frame with its alternative.
  **The shared guard caught two tools from the previous increment pointing at a SEALED endpoint**: `consultar_aip` and `eventos_preservacion` targeted the storage routes, which require `X-Internal-Token` — they would have returned `401` to everyone. The right ones are archive-service's **mediated** routes.
  191 mcp-server tests + 5 new friction tests, 17 shared guards, lint green.
  *Why*: without the instruments the assistant can process documents but cannot answer under which rules they are kept — the first question an archivist asks — *Impact*: 146 tools in total; F9 from 4 to 38, with friction placed on the datum whose error cannot be corrected.

- `feat(mcp)`: **preservation in the MCP, and why WORM sealing is NOT offered** (E18, F8)
  Closes the question left open when the confirmation ladder was built: which step does sealing an artefact fall on. The answer is not a step — it is that **it is not offered**, for a different reason than F7's `ejecutar`.
  In F7 the reason was the human guarantee. Here it is prior and simpler: **`proteger-indice` and `proteger-artefacto` require the `file_id` of the signed artefact, an identifier only archive-service composes in the expediente's context** — which is why the interface does not offer them either. A model would have to make it up, and sealing the wrong file under a ten-year lock cannot be undone: **retention cannot be shortened**. The card does not protect against that: it would show the person a UUID they have no way to check, and a card showing an uncheckable value is not a control, it is a formality.
  **Not offering the act cannot mean leaving people blind**: sealing happens on its own at closing, so what is offered is finding out — which indexes were left unsigned, which artefacts are near their retention expiry — and retrying the index signature.
  **Eight new tools**, and two distinctions that avoid spending friction where it is not needed: **format migration is NOT irreversible** (it creates a derivative and the original stays intact) and **declaring the preservation plan is not either** (append-only versioning); whereas **writing to the PREMIS record is**, even though it destroys nothing — the record admits no correction, so what is irreversible is **the assertion**.
  **And the census had an inflated denominator**: `/api/v1/storage/*` except `upload` is **not routed by the gateway**, and the `…-internal` twins are system triggers sealed with `X-Internal-Token`. Counting them as pending coverage made a properly closed service look like a gap. F8 goes from 24 operations to 11, and from 0% to **73%** — the three missing ones are the three declared as not offered.
  191 mcp-server tests (6 new), lint green across the 11 services, `mkdocs --strict` clean.
  *Why*: the question "which step does WORM sealing fall on?" had an answer that was not a step, and answering it with friction would have given a false sense of control over an invented identifier — *Impact*: 8 preservation tools, two more acts declared in `NO_OFRECIDAS`, a corrected census denominator, and 112 tools in total.

- `feat(mcp)`: **records disposal in the MCP, and the confirmation ladder it demanded** (E18, F7)
  F7 is the only procedure in the system that ends by **destroying archival records**: propose → publish → endorse → signed record of destruction → execute. Before exposing it, one question had to be answered that the catalogue had left open: **what must be confirmed**.
  **The gate had a single step** —the fixed phrase `CONFIRMO` for everything irreversible— and that is not enough, not because of severity but because of **object**: a fixed phrase can be typed from memory before reading anything, so it confirms "an act", not "this act". There are now three steps and a limit: (1) approve without typing, for the reversible; (2) `CONFIRMO`, for the irreversible without loss; (3) **the object's identifier, taken from the card**, for whatever enables a destruction; and (—) **do not offer it at all**.
  The third step is the leap: reproducing the batch identifier **cannot be anticipated** — you must have looked at the card. And what it does NOT do is stated in the code and the docs: it does not turn a model into a person; it raises the cost of carelessness, it does not prevent it.
  **That is why `ejecutar` is not offered.** It is the only path that deletes archival records and its human guarantee cannot depend on a third-party client having asked someone. The four prior phases are offered and **the absence is DECLARED** in the scope frame with its reason and its alternative path.
  **Friction runs the opposite way to the usual.** Withdrawing a batch —the act that STOPS the destruction— is the cheapest in all of F7. Endorsing, which enables it, is the most expensive. If stopping cost the same as proceeding, friction would push forward.
  **And the card summarises instead of dumping.** Forty UUIDs on a card do not inform, they **obscure**. Lists show as their size ("12 elements") and the detail still travels inside the signed proposal.
  10 F7 tools (0% → **91%**; only `ejecutar` missing, by decision). 185 mcp-server tests (12 new), mutation-red on both new mechanisms, 17 shared guards, lint green.
  *Why*: exposing deletion with the same ceremony as checking an inbox would have been this catalogue's worst possible mistake — *Impact*: `confirmar_con` in the catalogue, `frase_requerida()` in the gate, cards that summarise lists, `NO_OFRECIDAS` in the scope frame, and 106 tools in total.

- `feat(mcp)`: **F4, F5 and F6 go from almost nothing to covered** — expediente, physical storage and transfers, plus three more procedures (E18)
  Second increment on the workflow classification, in the order it set itself: F5 and F6 were the two workflows that **could not be carried out from the assistant** — an archivist cannot work without being able to shelve a box or close a transfer.
  **27 new tools.** F5 (7% → **79%**): locations, conservation units, placing an expediente with its folio range, movements and loan history. F6 (9% → **82%**): the whole custody cycle — create, send, receive, reject, the frozen record of delivery and its signature verification. F4 (32% → **61%**): exclude with cause, index as XML, integrity verification, versions, index signing, publication ex officio, access history and authorised groups.
  **The three acts that transfer custody** —send, receive, reject— are declared irreversible: they change who answers for the holdings. And **`preparar_transferencia` does not receive**, deliberately: receiving belongs to the receiving archive, and having one call do both ends would turn a handover between two responsible parties into a one-person errand.
  **Three new invariants, and all three found something.** That each step maps **only** arguments its tool declares —the real error was writing `dest_dept`, the *endpoint's* name, where the tool declares `dest_dept_code`—; that it supplies **all** required ones; and that **no parameter asked of the person goes unused**. The third uncovered a defect of mine: `responder_radicado` asked for the originating radicado **and never linked it**. Its description promised a link the draft circuit does not make, because the system creates the antecedent when CREATING the outgoing document, not afterwards. Renamed to `proyectar_y_radicar_respuesta`, with a description that tells the truth.
  **And the same defect was in the corpus**: `seed_corpus.py` read `GET /expedientes/{id}/radicados`, a route **that does not exist**, with `opcional=True` — the 404 read as "it has none" and every pass refilled already-full expedientes. Third appearance of the same pattern in two days: **an optional read turns "I could not read" into "there is nothing"**.
  Verified against the live stack with corpus data: 103 tools visible to `operador` (96 tools + 7 procedures), the physical-storage ones return the 9 units and 6 locations seeded, and `estado_del_expediente` composes four queries into one over a real expediente.
  173 mcp-server tests (3 new, mutation-verified), 17 shared guards, lint green across the 11 services, `mkdocs --strict` clean.
  *Why*: a workflow classification is not worth much as a document unless it becomes the tools that were missing, and the two least-covered workflows were exactly the ones leaving an archivist unable to work — *Impact*: 96 tools (from 69) and 7 procedures (from 4); total coverage 92 → 96 of 367 operations, concentrated in the six workflows a civil servant walks.

- `feat(mcp,shared)`: **the API classified by WORKFLOWS, and 24 new tools derived from that classification** (E18)
  Requested by the user: *"classify by workflows in order to create the MCP tools"*. A service map tells you **who serves** each endpoint; what has to be decided is **what someone doing a job needs** — and a front-desk procedure touches four services. Hence the unit of analysis is the workflow, not the microservice.
  **Fourteen workflows**, censused mechanically by crossing the gateway OpenAPI with the MCP catalogue (`scripts/censo_mcp.py`, reproducible, with `--detalle F4` for an operation-by-operation view). The document lives at `docs/es/mcp-flujos.md`.
  **What stays OUT is declared with its reason**, because a census counting all 367 as debt would lie: `/internal/*` is sealed service-to-service; `/api/v1/tenants/*` is **deliberately unrouted** (the tenant registry lives in `public` and any user of one institution could write another's); `/api/v1/public/*` is the citizen window, session-less, while the MCP always acts on behalf of an authenticated officer. **And the census states its own limit**: the gateway aggregator composes seven of the eleven services, so 367 is a floor.
  **24 new tools**, in the three workflows a civil servant walks daily: F1 front desk (third parties with the DIVIPOLA cascade, frozen actors, motivated reclassification, publication ex officio and the whole annulment circuit), F2 routing (return with cause, approval chains, events, roll back an assignment) and F3 dispatch (shipment inbox, dispatch, status update). Coverage across those three goes from 24% to 61%.
  **Permissions were not declared by eye**: they were extracted from the real `require_permission` of each router. An invented permission makes a tool invisible to everyone but ROOT, and that already happened in this catalogue with a `RADI_CREAR` that did not exist.
  **The guard meant to prevent that was half blind, and that is the deeper fix.** `shared/tests/test_permisos_declarados.py` read only the DECORATOR's `require_permission`, not the router's own. It cut both ways: it accused the five third-party tools of over-declaring —their router does declare `PERM_RADI`— and, worse, **it would not have caught a tool declaring LESS than a router-level gate**, which is precisely what the guard exists to prevent. It also kept **one prefix per file**: in `anulacion.py`, which has two routers, the first router's endpoints hung off a foreign prefix and matched nothing — four tools went unchecked and it only surfaced because the overall count dropped. Fixed router by router, and verified by mutation.
  Verified against the live stack with corpus data: 73 tools visible to user `operador`, `listar_departamentos` returns the DANE's 33 and `buscar_terceros` finds the seeded ones.
  170 mcp-server tests, 17 shared guards, lint green across the 11 services.
  *Why*: grouping by service answers who serves an endpoint, not what the worker needs; and a catalogue whose guard cannot see half the gates can lie about permissions without anything failing — *Impact*: new `docs/es/mcp-flujos.md` and `scripts/censo_mcp.py`, 24 more tools (69 total + 4 procedures), and the permission cross-check now reads router-level gates too.

- `feat(mcp,gateway,scripts)`: **a real test corpus, and the MCP starts offering PROCEDURES rather than isolated calls** (E18, ADR-019)
  Requested by the user: before touching the MCP, the system needed a database populated well enough to test it whole. `scripts/seed_corpus.py` now seeds **197 radicados** (150 annexes across 78 of them, 28 reserved and 22 classified), 16 expedientes with deliberately uneven contents —24, 21, 20, 20, 12, 10, 7, 7, 5, 4, 3, 3, 2, 1, 1 and **one empty**, the edge screens tend to forget—, 40 third parties with DIVIPOLA geography spread across the country, 41 postal shipments in various states, and physical storage with an outstanding loan.
  **Everything through the API, never through SQL.** A direct `INSERT` bypasses atomic numbering, the business rules and the `audit_log` hash chain — in this installation the only non-conforming audit rows are, precisely, raw-SQL fixtures. And it **converges**: two runs leave the same archive, not a bigger one; a corpus that grows on every pass consumes radicado numbers, which are immutable and never returned.
  **Seeding uncovered two defects no test had touched.** `POST /api/v1/terceros` answered `route_not_found`: **the prefix was missing from the gateway routing table**, so third-party registration and the DIVIPOLA cascade —built, unit-tested and documented— were unreachable from the frontend. And migration `tenant/027` had not been applied to the deployment: the `tercero` table did not exist. A capability built and not offered, again, this time with two stacked causes.
  **Three defects in the script itself, and the costliest was mine**: it asked for `size=200` where the cap is 100, the resulting 422 fell into a read declared "optional", and "I could not read" was taken as "it does not exist" — every run recreated what already existed. The fix is not raising the cap: it is that **a convergence check cannot be optional**. Assuming emptiness is exactly what produces duplicates, and silently. The other two: the Keycloak token expired mid-run on a long seed (now renewed once per request), and reused expedientes stayed empty because "converge" had been read as "do nothing if they already exist".
  **The MCP: 367 gateway operations, 41 covered.** The census was done by crossing the aggregated OpenAPI against the catalogue, not by reading: 11% coverage. This increment does not close that gap —it is stated and is the work that follows— but it fixes what made it useless: **the MCP offered isolated calls while a civil servant performs procedures**. Registering a petition is four acts, not one; with isolated tools the model picks the order and links the steps, and when something fails halfway nobody knows what state the matter is in.
  **Procedures** declare that sequence server-side, in the same declarative shape as the catalogue (an entry, not code per procedure): `tramitar_peticion_ciudadana`, `responder_radicado`, `conformar_expediente` and `estado_del_tramite`. Three properties make them safe: **every step carries the person's token** —a procedure is an ordering, not a credential: it can do nothing its user cannot—; **one confirmation for the whole act**, because asking four in a row teaches people to confirm without reading; and **atomicity is not faked**.
  That last point is the core. No transaction spans HTTP calls to different services, and even if one did, **registering consumes an immutable number that never returns to the counter**. So the result is a step-by-step report, not a boolean. Verified live, and by the best kind of accident: while testing it I typed "Oficina Juridica" without its accent, radicado `2026-DEMO-E-000127` was created and the routing step failed with `422`; the response said `pasos_ejecutados: ['radicar']` and warned against repeating the whole thing. A bare "it failed" would have led to registering the same petition twice — the costliest and least reversible damage this system can do.
