Metadata is **replaced wholesale**, not merged (a merge leaves residue from the old type, which is exactly what makes an expediente stop adding up), and the template version used is rewritten whenever re-validation happens — **including the `NULL`** when the new type has no active template; without that explicit `NULL` the radicado would keep pointing at the previous type's template. Sending only `metadata` re-validates too: otherwise registration's mandatory validation would be bypassable with a later PATCH. **The new act gets its own gate without touching the existing ones**: the PATCH is gated with `PERM_RADI` at level 1 — read — and stays that way, because raising it wholesale would have broken everyone who today corrects a subject or a page count, and aligning that threshold is a decision for the epic, not a side effect of this change. But `doc_class` decides how long the document is kept and whether it is ultimately destroyed, so it is checked separately at `crud >= 3`. **A defect of my own, caught by my own tests**: I added the failure mode (metadata validation) without its response — `MetadataValidationError` was not in the PATCH's `except`, only the POST's, so validation surfaced as a **500**. A validation without its response mapping is not a validation, it is a crash. **And a test that did not discriminate**, caught by mutation: it checked that `metadata_template_id` appeared in the `UPDATE` SQL, and found it in the `RETURNING` clause — green with the write in place **and** removed. Corrected to `metadata_template_id = $N` plus checking the value sent. Same pattern yet again: the searched string appears elsewhere in the same text. — *Why*: the document type is not a descriptive field, it is what binds the radicado to its TRD series; leaving it immutable by omission turns a counter mistake into the wrong retention for the document's entire life — *Impact*: `PATCH /api/v1/documents/{id}` accepts `doc_class` and `metadata`; `422 metadata_validation_failed` when they do not fit; `403` when typing without creation level. New MCP tools `tipificar_radicado` (write, `min_crud=3`, with its declared alternative route) and `listar_tipos_documentales`, which is where the name the former expects comes from. Rebuild the document-service and mcp-server images. Verified by mutation, each control failing its own test: removing the level gate, removing the re-validation, not rewriting the template version, and dropping `doc_class` from the audited fields.

- `feat(mcp)`: **confirming "something" was not confirming "this" — writes become a signed proposal** — the MCP write gate was `X-Confirm-Write: true`: a boolean any client could set, bound to no particular act. It counted the same for a query as for registering an official document. The MCP binding simply assumed it (a hardcoded `confirm_write=True`, "because the human-in-the-loop is supplied by the MCP client" — a guarantee turned into an assumption about someone else's software), while the assistant forbade writes outright. **Three different behaviours depending on which door you came through** guarantees one of them is wrong, and the wrong one was the least-watched. There is now **one gate for all three transports**: invoking a write **returns a proposal and executes nothing**. The proposal is an opaque HMAC-SHA256-signed token carrying the complete act — tool and already-validated arguments — and on confirmation the arguments are taken **from the token**, never from the request body: "what was signed" is "what runs" by construction, not by discipline. Returning a proposal **instead of a 412** is deliberate: a legacy or hostile client that fabricates any header gets a description of the act, not an effect. **Signed rather than stored in memory** because the service's only store is a per-process dictionary, declared invalid for multiple replicas: with memory, proposing on one replica and confirming on another would break the button the person did press. **Re-authorisation on confirmation is not redundant**: between proposing and confirming lie the seconds it takes someone to read the card, and the permission may have been revoked in that gap — a proposal attests *what* was approved, not that whoever presents it may still do it; confusing the two is what turns a token into a free pass. And **the card is worded by the catalogue, not the model**: generated text could misdescribe the act being signed, which would be social engineering aimed at the very person confirming it. For **external MCP clients**, which have neither card nor endpoint of their own: a generic `confirmar_accion(token)` tool (offered only to users who can reach some write — suggesting it to someone who cannot write implies a power they do not have), standard `annotations` (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`), and in-band **elicitation** where the transport is bidirectional (stdio); elicitation approval does not execute by a different route — it goes through the same signed token, so it re-authorises and consumes identically. **Two defects surfaced while implementing it**, both of the "mechanism that reports success without verifying anything" kind: the execution endpoint **was never actually inserted** (a `grep` for a string that also appeared in a comment vouched for something that did not exist; verification is now by defined symbol), and proposals issued over the MCP transport **were dead on arrival** — signed with a session-dictionary key that never existed, so `sub` went out empty and verification later rejected them as belonging to someone else: an immaculate-looking, unconfirmable proposal whose failure only showed when the button was pressed. `destructive` is declared **explicitly** on every write tool, defaulting to `None` on purpose: with a `False` default, forgetting it would have amounted to asserting "this act can be undone" with nobody having checked, and an external client would have lowered its friction trusting that phantom claim. An invariant enforces it. — *Why*: a boolean in a header binds confirmation to no act, and a guarantee that depends on the client asking is not a guarantee — *Impact*: `X-Confirm-Write` is no longer read (senders get a proposal anyway). New `POST /api/v1/mcp/proposals/execute` and `POST /api/v1/assistant/confirmar` (the latter under `/assistant/` because it is the only mcp-server prefix the gateway routes). `MCP_PROPOSAL_SECRET` is **mandatory**: the service refuses to start without it (a signature with an empty key is reproducible by anyone and would leave the gate decorative). This requires building `mcp-server` with the repo root as context so `shared/` is installed. Rebuild its image. *Declared limits*: consumption by `jti` lives in process memory — it covers double-clicks and retries, not multi-replica — and `Idempotency-Key` is emitted as a hook, not a guarantee; over streamable HTTP (`stateless`) there is no return channel to elicit on, leaving the two-tool path.

- `fix(mcp)`: **a path parameter containing `../` invoked a different endpoint with the user's token** — the dispatcher interpolated path parameters with a raw `str()`. A `document_id` of `../../auth/me` did not produce a 404: it made the mcp-server **call another gateway endpoint with the user's token**. Same normalisation defect that block 1 of the production plan closed in the gateway, alive one hop further in — and the reason the dispatcher review moved up in priority. Closed with `quote(safe="")`, in two layers: `coerce_args` validates `format="uuid"` first, so most such values never reach the escape. **The dispatcher only knew GET and POST**: anything that was not GET fell into an unconditional POST. Most of the system's writes are `PATCH`, and there are `PUT` and `DELETE`, so the catalogue could not express half the operations. There is now `build_request` (prepares, does not send — which will let us sign exactly what gets executed) and `execute`, with all four methods and argument placement by `location`. `DELETE` never carries a body: some proxies silently drop it, and a request whose meaning depends on something that may vanish in transit is not a reliable request. The argument whitelist moves from the assistant to the **dispatcher**, so all three transports inherit it. **A test detail that matters more than the change**: moving dispatch from `client.get`/`client.post` to `client.request` would have left the security assertions saying `post.assert_not_called()` **meaningless without failing** — the write gate and the assistant's defence in depth would have been watching a method nobody uses any more. They were migrated to `request`, now exclusive to dispatches (context reads stay on `.get`), so the assertion literally says "no tool was dispatched" and does not hollow out when a tool changes method. — *Why*: a layer that translates names into routes and does not escape what it interpolates is not a façade, it is an inward-facing open proxy — *Impact*: mcp-server only; no tool changes contract. Rebuild its image.

- `feat(mcp)`: **the assistant now states what you can do before you start, instead of letting you find out by failing** — foundations of the functional MCP layer (plan blocks 0, 1 and A). All three fix the same shape of defect: a capability that exists in the code and nobody can use, with nothing complaining. **The MCP's only write had been switched off since its first commit**: `radicar_documento` declared the permission `RADI_CREAR`, which **does not exist in the system** — it is in none of the five migrations that seed `auth_permissions`; the real one is `PERM_RADI`. `visible_tools` compared against a name that never appears in `/auth/me`'s set, so for every non-ROOT user the tool was invisible and invoking it returned 403. The service's own tests missed it because they **fabricated the permission by hand**, self-consistent with the defect. An invariant now reads the real permissions from the migrations and fails if the catalogue declares one that does not exist. **Identity was resolved three ways and two did not work**: the HTTP router and the MCP binding read `X-User-Permissions`, a header **nobody produces** (the gateway injects six others and drops that one). Unified in `app/services/identity.py`, always from the validated token. Visibility now respects the **CRUD level** (real 0..5 scale), not just permission presence: holding `PERM_RADI` to read no longer enables what requires create — and asking for more hides the tool from those who can, which fails silently. **`_TOOL_ARGS` is gone**, the parameter table parallel to the catalogue; a tool with no entry there and no path parameters was dispatched **with no arguments at all**. Migrating against the real routers surfaced a drift — `buscar_conocimiento` advertised `query` of minimum 2 characters and `top_k` up to 20 when the backend accepts 1 and 50 — with a test freezing it. `query` is aligned; `top_k` stays at 20 but now **said out loud**: an assistant-side limit to avoid flooding its context, not a system one. **The scope frame** (`GET /api/v1/assistant/alcance`, a panel in the dock, injected into the prompt): what this person can do, can consult, and **cannot** — with **their alternative route**: "you cannot send it, but you can transfer the radicado to correspondence so they send it". It is **derived** from the catalogue crossed with `/auth/me`, `/auth/clearance` and `/auth/context`, so adding a tool updates the frame by itself. And it declares what the system does **not** distinguish, to avoid promising precision it lacks: the registration permission does not separate by document type (`PERM_RADI_ENTRADA`/`_INTERNO` exist and no gate uses them), and reads are bounded by security level, not by dependencia. — *Why*: a permission you only discover by failing wastes two people's time and turns a legitimate limit into an apparent malfunction; and a refusal without an alternative leaves the asker exactly as stuck — *Impact*: the assistant is still **read-only** — writes arrive with the confirmation gate of the following blocks — but it now states its scope. Rebuild the mcp-server and frontend images.

- `fix(tooling)`: **the lint gate could fail without saying why** — surfaced by running it while another instance was in flight: **four** services red, two of them with no changes at all, and not a single error message to read. The code was not at fault: the in-container `ruff`/`mypy` install ran with `2>/dev/null` under `set -e`, so any failure of that install — network, contention from two concurrent gates, a slow package index — aborted the service and listed it as `FAILED` **with no visible cause**. Notably, the lesson was already written **two lines below**: the `shared/` install carries a comment explaining it is deliberately not silenced, because a gate that quietly reduces its own coverage is worse than a red one. It had been applied to half the file. — *Why*: a gate that produces unexplainable red trains you to ignore it — the same failure mode as the pipeline that never ran the tests and the health check that returned a fixed 200 — *Impact*: tooling only. Run in isolation, all eleven services pass.

- `feat(archive,frontend)`: **the publication act reaches expedientes — the last art. 12 asymmetry** — closes the item both auditors independently flagged. The art. 12 correction had been applied to radicados and not here: `archive-service` still carried `_PUBLIC = "nivel_seguridad = 1"`, so the multi-level EAD OAI kept proactively disseminating the code, name, description, extreme dates and radicado count of **every unclassified expediente** — the column's default. The asymmetry was also implicit, which is the worst kind: anyone reading document-service's cut would assume this service did the same. Migration `042` (`publicable` defaulting to FALSE plus `publicado_at`/`publicado_por` and a partial index), a cut requiring both conditions, `POST /expedientes/{id}/publicacion` with `PERM_RECLASIFICAR` at `crud≥3` and a justification mandatory in both directions, act and audit entry in a single transaction, and the state exposed in `ExpedienteResponse`. The screen — a "Publicación" drawer in `/expedientes/{id}` — ships **in the same change**: closing the channel without leaving the legitimate door is the very defect this work fixed two commits ago. **Two deliberate differences from radicados.** The cut carries **no status condition**: an annulled radicado is withdrawn from circulation, but a `closed`/`transferred` expediente is not — those are normal lifecycle states — and copying `status <> …` for symmetry would have silently unpublished the whole closed archive, which is most of a fonds. And the level gate applies **only when publishing**: over a reserved expediente the screen does not offer publishing — and explains why — but does offer withdrawal, which is where it matters most. The radicado twin defect does not exist here: **there is no expediente reclassification** — `nivel_seguridad` is set at creation and no route mutates it (`ExpedienteUpdate` is `extra="forbid"`), so no level change can undo a publication. — *Why*: a publication scheme governing one object type and not the other is not a scheme, it is an exception — *Impact*: **existing expedientes become non-publishable** after migration `042`. An institution using the expediente OAI must explicitly publish what it wants disseminated. Rebuild the archive-service and frontend images.

- `fix(document,shared,ci)`: **the publication act governed metadata but not the bytes, and a reclassification undid it** — findings from a cross audit (multi-tenant security + archival compliance), with both auditors independently converging on the same two blockers. All three defects are **scope** defects, not design ones: the pattern "explicit act + justification + audit entry in the same transaction + gate inside the `WHERE`" is right; what failed was not applying it everywhere it belonged. **(1) CMIS served content without requiring the act.** The `_PUBLIC` chokepoint gained `publicable` in block 11, but `get_public_anexo` — which feeds `getContentStream`, i.e. the **attachment bytes** — carried a hand-written copy of the `WHERE` that was never updated, and its docstring claimed to anchor on `_PUBLIC`, which made the divergence invisible on reading. With public interoperability enabled, anyone on the internet could download the attachment of any unclassified, non-annulled radicado the entity had never published — and tracking numbers are consecutive, so the channel is enumerable. Closed with `_public(alias)`: a chokepoint that is *built*, not rewritten by hand. **(2) Classifying left the publication latent and declassifying revived it.** `set_classification` did not touch `publicable`: publish → classify (the flag stays TRUE, harmless while the cut requires both) → declassify months later for an unrelated reason → **the radicado returns to the harvesting channels with no act, no justification and no audit entry**. Classifying now withdraws the publication in the same transaction as its audit entry, because a reservation is a reasoned act that prevails (Ley 1712 arts. 18-19). **(3) And an already-classified radicado could not be unpublished** — the `UPDATE` required `nivel_seguridad = 1` in *both* directions, so the state where withdrawal matters most was the only one without an exit. The gate is now conditional on direction. `set_nivel_seguridad` is **removed** (no production callers, and it did exactly the dangerous thing), along with the test comment describing it as current. **(4) The single migration ledger self-attributed ambiguous names**: a service runner sees only its own directories, so for one of the four colliding filenames it saw a single candidate — itself — and marked it applied: block 11's silent skip, now fossilised with full identity. Fixed with `NOMBRES_AMBIGUOS` plus a test that **recomputes the set from the repository** and fails if it diverges. **(5) `shared/tests/` never ran in CI**: the `test-unit` matrix enumerates the eleven services and the shared library is not a service, so 61 tests covering the immutable audit log, the event envelope, fail-fast secrets and the migration ledger fell through the enumeration gap — the same failure mode as the type gate that covered 8 of 11. New `test-unit-shared` job. — *Why*: a publication opt-in that governs metadata but not content is not an opt-in; and an act another act can undo without justification is not an act — *Impact*: **no migration**. Radicados published and later classified keep the flag in the database: a sanitising `UPDATE` (`publicable = FALSE WHERE nivel_seguridad > 1`) is advisable before enabling public interoperability. Rebuild the document-service image.

- `fix(document,frontend)`: **the publication act had no screen, and its result could not be read** — closing block 11 changed `publicable`'s default to `FALSE` — correctly, because publishing is a decision of the entity and not the consequence of not having classified (Ley 1712/2014 art. 12). What was missing was the legitimate door: `POST /documents/{id}/publicacion` existed, the gateway routed it, and **no screen called it**. An institution that updated was left with every radicado non-publishable and no way to publish any of them from the application: the publication scheme became unexecutable except through SQL. A leak was closed and the system was left without the door. A second defect surfaced while building the screen: `publicable` could be **written but not read** — it was in neither the radicado detail nor the listing, so neither could the UI offer the act over a known state nor could the entity attest through the API to what it had published. A publication scheme that cannot be consulted is not a scheme, it is a blind write; the mapping crosses three layers (SQL query → domain object → response) and the field was missing in all three. The screen is a side panel in the tray that reads the state from the server and always offers the act **opposite** to the current one — publish or withdraw — with a mandatory justification in both directions and an explicit warning of what publishing does: the radicado becomes harvestable over OAI-PMH and CMIS. Over a reserved or annulled radicado **no form is offered**: the backend answers with a neutral `404` (deliberately indistinguishable from "does not exist", so as not to be an oracle over classified material), and letting someone draft a justification only to receive "does not exist" afterwards would be a screen that deceives. The button requires the backend's real threshold — `PERM_RECLASIFICAR` at write level: asking for less produces a 403 on click, asking for more hides it from those who may act. — *Why*: a decision the law requires an entity to make, and to attest to afterwards, cannot depend on direct database access — *Impact*: radicados are still born non-publishable; there is now a way to publish them and a way to consult what was published. Rebuild the document-service and frontend images.

- `fix(shared,gateway,document,archive,auth,storage,infra)`: **publishing stopped being a decision, and an incident could not be followed end to end (production audit, block 11)** — **What gets published is now decided, not inferred.** Two harvesting channels let other institutions and the public collect the entity's records. Until now anything not marked as reserved went out through them — that is, **having decided nothing was equivalent to having decided to publish**: a freshly registered radicado, or one declassified for an unrelated purpose, left on its own. Ley 1712/2014 art. 12 asks for the opposite: a publication scheme, an act by which the entity decides what it disseminates. Each radicado now carries a publication flag that starts **off** and only turns on through an explicit, justified and recorded act, stamped with who did it and when; the `UPDATE` demands `nivel_seguridad = 1` in its own `WHERE`, so a false claim cannot be stored that the next declassification would turn into publication. **An incident could not be traced across services.** `X-Request-Id` had always been on the forwarded-header list — and the gateway generated it in the **response** middleware, after proxying: the pipe was laid and nothing flowed through it. It is now assigned on the way in (pure ASGI, so block 4's chunked streaming survives), travels through every service, comes back to the browser, and each service writes one structured line with entity, user, operation and duration — never the query string. **Example passwords that actually worked.** This repository is public, and two secrets — the electronic-signature key and the object-store credentials — shipped with a **working** example value: an institution that forgot to change them got no warning. Both now default to `""` and the service **refuses to start** without them. The identity-server sample config, which contained two published-password users (one an administrator), is renamed to `realm-dev.json`, and production starts from a `realm-prod.json.example` template that cannot be used unfilled. **CORS `*` with credentials was hardcoded.** Starlette cannot answer `*` with credentials, so it reflects the incoming `Origin` — that is not permissive, it is CSRF. It is now off by default (the product does not need it) and whoever opens it must enumerate the origins (`CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS`). **The deployment guide told you to copy a template that did not exist**: `infra/nginx.conf.example` is now included and validated with a real `nginx -t`, with HSTS and `X-Forwarded-Proto/Host/For` — without it, login did not complete, the IP sealed into the immutable audit trail was the reverse proxy's own, and large downloads buffered in memory again. **Migration bookkeeping was kept in duplicate.** Two runners (`services/*/migrations/runner.py` and `init_tenant.py`) recorded applied work in three registries invisible to each other, keyed by the **bare filename** — and each service numbers its own series from 001, so four names collide (`002_batch_jobs.sql` between archive and document; the `005`/`006`/`007` dead-letter ones between notification and signature). The second service's runner therefore saw the name and **skipped a migration of its own that had never been applied** — silently, with no error. Harmless so far by coincidence (all four are idempotent and nearly identical). There is now a single ledger, `public.schema_migrations`, keyed by schema + service + filename, which imports the three old registries without re-applying anything; an ambiguous bare name is attributed to the first candidate **with a warning**, because silencing it would repeat the defect. **The `audit_log` was never partitioned by month**: migration 002 assumed a job nobody had written, everything piled into `audit_log_default`, and PostgreSQL refuses to create a partition whose range overlaps rows already in the default — the trap tightens over time. `ensure_audit_partitions` now creates the coming months' partitions **and hardens each one against deletion**: the immutability guards are per-table and a new partition does not inherit them. **Three missing access controls**: (1) `POST /ingest/email/poll` required only a valid JWT, and since the process has a single IMAP mailbox with the target entity taken from the header, a user of entity A could pull mail addressed to B into their own — and the poll marks it read, so it never comes back: not an undue read, an irreversible appropriation. The mailbox now declares its owning entity (`IMAP_TENANT_SLUG`, fail-closed) and the endpoint requires `USUA_PERM_ADMIN`, leaving an audit entry per poll; (2) `PATCH /documents/{id}/disposition` set «eliminar» with **read** permission — now `min_crud=3`, with a mandatory rationale, written in the same transaction as its audit entry; (3) the FUID went out with no header: `entidad_productora` is now resolved server-side from `public.tenants`, `unidad_administrativa`/`oficina_productora`/`objeto` are declared per report, and consultation frequency is computed from real accesses in the `audit_log` scoped by `tenant_slug` (the only table shared across entities) instead of estimated. — *Why*: publishing public information is a decision of the entity, not a side effect of not having classified; and a working example key is a known key — *Impact*: **existing radicados become non-publishable** until someone performs the publication act, so an institution using the harvesting channels must expressly publish what it wants disseminated; three environment variables become mandatory before deploying (`SIGNATURE_SECRET`, `MINIO_ACCESS_KEY`, `MINIO_SECRET_KEY`), plus `IMAP_TENANT_SLUG` if email ingestion is used.

- `fix(shared,gateway,*)`: **automatic mail distribution could stop silently while the system kept reporting it was fine (production audit, blocks 9–10)** — four background consumers react to what happens in the system: distributing incoming correspondence, sending email, sealing signatures and feeding the search index. If the internal message broker was unavailable at the exact moment of startup — or restarted afterwards — those consumers **died and never came back**. The system looked healthy: you could register, query and sign. But new documents stopped reaching dependencias, mail did not go out and search kept answering with stale data. Nothing warned. They now restart themselves with exponential backoff, and every failure is recorded. **The health check returned an automatic "all good"**: three services reported healthy **without checking anything** — they said so with the database down. The opposite was also wrong: the gateway declared itself unhealthy when **any** service behind it failed, so the orchestrator restarted the one component still able to serve. There are now two distinct probes — liveness (`/health`, which governs restarts) and a new readiness probe reporting the real state of database, broker, mail and consumers, so the load balancer stops routing to a node that cannot serve without restarting it. Those probes also **leaked internal hostnames and ports** in their error text to unauthenticated callers; they now report state in one word, with the detail left in the server log. **Login failures with no apparent cause**: the pool asked PostgreSQL for more concurrent connections than it grants, on a server shared with the identity system — so under documentary load what failed was **signing in**. The arithmetic is now done, written down and tuned, and a stuck query no longer holds its connection indefinitely. — *Why*: a consumer that dies silently is official correspondence that stops being distributed with nobody noticing, and a health check that always says "fine" disables the very monitoring it appears to provide — *Impact*: images must be rebuilt and the updated production manifest deployed (it tunes the database configuration); anyone monitoring `/health` expecting dependency state must move to `/health/ready`.

- `fix(frontend)`: **hovering the sign-out icon logged you out (production audit, block 9)** — sign-out was a link, and the framework preloads a page's work **when the pointer passes over it**; since the logout happened during that preload, brushing the icon — without clicking — really ended the session. Worse: because the effect lived in a plain GET, a link or an image embedded in an email triggered it too. It now requires pressing a button, and typing the URL by hand lands on a confirmation screen. **Error messages exposed the institution's internal network**: hostnames, addresses and ports appeared on screen; the user now gets a clear message and a **reference code** to quote to support, with the technical detail confined to the server log, plus a branded error page (a broken link used to land on a blank page with raw text and no way back). **The UI could wait forever**: with no client timeout, screens neither errored nor recovered when the backend was slow — including the login screen. There is now a maximum wait, with large downloads (expediente export, preservation package, FUID) explicitly exempt: what gets cut off is a server that does not answer, not one that is sending a big file. **The Back button showed the previous user's tray**: the browser replayed cached pages, so at a shared counter the next officer could see the previous one's radicados and subjects — including classified documents they are not cleared for — and signing out did not prevent it. No screen carrying working data is cached now. A **Content-Security-Policy** is added as a second browser-level barrier against script injected through a document, a template or an assistant reply, even if the existing sanitisation failed. Finally, when permissions fail to load the application **says so**: the menu used to just render empty, indistinguishable from having had permissions revoked — the officer called the administrator, who saw their permissions intact and had nothing to fix. — *Why*: an action with consequences must not happen on hover, and a screen that hides the fact that it is half-working wastes two people's time — *Impact*: sign-out behaves the same when clicked; the frontend image must be rebuilt on deploy.

- `chore(deps,docker)`: **dependencies brought up to date and services stop running as root (production audit, block 8)** — the libraries of all eleven services were updated, including cryptography, the web server and **the one that signs the electronic index**; that is why the change was not accepted by reading the list: the 2,500+ tests and the real-database suites were run first. The frontend moves to Node 22 — the previous line lost support in April 2025 and was what shipped — and to the latest framework release, which carries the request-size fix from the previous block. Ten of the eleven services ran **as root inside their container**; all of them process input they do not control (documents, mail, forms), so a flaw in any library handed over the whole container rather than just the process. They now run as an unprivileged user. And the object-store image stops tracking the `latest` tag: irreversible retention protection lives there, and having it change by itself in a routine update is not acceptable. — *Why*: running as root voids container isolation exactly where untrusted input is processed, and an unsupported release gets no security fixes — *Impact*: no visible change in use; images must be rebuilt on deploy.

- `fix(gateway,auth,frontend)`: **the IP address recorded in the audit trail was chosen by the visitor (production audit, block 7)** — the system trusted a header anyone can write to decide where each request came from. Two consequences: rate limiting was evaded by changing it on each attempt and — worse — **the address stamped into the immutable, evidentiary `audit_log`** was whatever the visitor put there. A chain-of-custody field that can be forged is worse than an empty one, because the empty one deceives nobody. The address is now derived server-side by walking the chain of **declared trusted** proxies and passed internally over a path the visitor cannot alter; with no proxies declared the header is ignored entirely — a useless datum is preferable to a false one. A day-to-day defect went with it: **every user's requests counted as coming from the same address**, because the frontend did not forward the browser's, producing "too many requests" lockouts in an ordinary morning at the counter; and public PQRS intake gets its own stricter limit, since it creates radicados unauthenticated and **consumes numbers that are never reused**. Finally, the audit trail stops writing one entry **per request** to say the token was valid: that buried the facts that matter — accesses, signatures, reclassifications — under enormous noise, making the log unreadable for the very person who comes to investigate. Invalid-token attempts are still recorded. — *Why*: an evidentiary log where nearly every row is noise proves nothing, and neither does an address the visitor picks — *Impact*: the institution must declare its network intermediaries on deploy (`TRUSTED_PROXIES`); without it, per-address limiting does not distinguish users.

- `fix(ci)`: **the automated tests had never run in continuous integration (production audit, block 6)** — the pipeline meant to verify every change died on its **first line**, because of a configuration difference between how it invoked the lint gate and how the project does; since the tool stops at the first failure, **the test run never happened at all** — and the pipeline had been giving the opposite impression for a long time. CI now runs **exactly the same gate** a developer runs locally instead of a copy free to diverge; it runs the eleven services' suites in parallel; and it gains two lanes that did not exist: **real-database** integration tests (256 tests that were written but had never been executed) and **frontend** tests, which had none. Version-tagged image publishing is added too — that is what makes rolling back a failed update possible. Actually running it surfaced three problems reading had not revealed, two of them in the fix itself; they were corrected before accepting it. — *Why*: a pipeline that does not run the tests is not verification, it is decoration — and it grants confidence it has not earned — *Impact*: CI only; no change in system behaviour.

- `feat(infra)`: **backup and restore now really exist, and have been exercised end to end (production audit, block 5)** — the backup procedure lived only as an example inside the guide, and that example **did not work**: it connected as a test user, copied files to a directory that does not exist and — worst — if the dump failed it still produced an empty file **and deleted the previous backups**. Run nightly by cron, that means accumulating months of empty copies that look normal. The recovery procedure, for its part, shut the database down and then tried to restore onto it. There are now two real programs. **Backup** copies database and documents, **verifies the copy can be read** before accepting it, and only then rotates the old ones; if anything is interrupted the copy is marked incomplete and restore refuses to use it. **Restore** requires written confirmation and, on finishing, **verifies the audit trail's hash chain**: that the database starts does not prove the documentary trace is intact, and that is precisely what an entity must be able to attest. If something was tampered with, it says so and points at the entry. The full cycle — copy, destroy, restore, verify — has been executed against a real environment, and the verification was itself put to the test by deliberately altering an entry: it catches it. The **upgrade and rollback** procedure is added too, including the warning that rolling the program back does not roll back schema changes — that is what the backup is for. — *Why*: a backup whose restore has never been tested is not a backup, it is a file; and no institution should put official records into a system without that guarantee — *Impact*: deployment and operations only.

- `fix(gateway,storage,frontend)`: **scanned documents can finally be attached: anything over 512 KB used to fail (production audit, block 4)** — the frontend server shipped a **512 KB** per-request body limit that had never been configured, and since attachments pass through it **any real scanned PDF was rejected** with a terse "upload failed". Bulk expediente import was outright unusable. Raising the limit alone would have been worse: each file was buffered **whole in memory** twice — once at the gateway and once in storage-service — with no size cap at all, so a large file would not have errored, it would have taken the service down. The change therefore does three things at once: files are now streamed **in chunks** without accumulating in memory, there is an explicit cap that returns a clear message when exceeded, and the frontend limit goes to 512 MB. — *Why*: a records-management system where a scanned document cannot be attached does not do its job — *Impact*: attachments and bulk import work; a file above the cap now gets an explicit error stating the maximum size instead of a generic failure.

- `fix(document)`: **editing a radicado now leaves a trace, and an annulment is not undone by editing a field (production audit, block 3)** — changing a radicado's subject, addressee, status or folio count was recorded nowhere — striking, because **reading** it was. An official document with an immutable number that can be edited without leaving a trace does not meet what the documentary trace requires. Moreover an **annulled** radicado — a decision that goes through request and supervisor approval — could return to active status through a plain edit, with no cause, no approver and no record. The system now rejects that and explains why: reversing an annulment, if it is ever warranted, is an act of its own with its own justification, not the side effect of editing a field. Each modification now records **which field changed, from what value to what value**, along with who did it; and if that record cannot be written for any reason, the modification **is not applied** — a change failing is preferable to a change happening untraced. — *Why*: an archival document's traceability admits no gaps, and an annulment undone through a side path is not an annulment — *Impact*: attempting to modify an annulled radicado now returns an explicit error.

- `fix(document,archive)`: **the correspondence register stopped being public by default (production audit, block 2)** — the two harvesting channels (OAI-PMH and CMIS), meant for other institutions and open-data portals to consult what the entity has decided to publish, were **enabled from first boot**, and what they exposed depended on each document being classified as reserved: since a radicado's default is "public", the practice was "everything is published unless somebody remembers". Out went each radicado's number, **subject** and dependencia, and attachment content could be downloaded **with no record of who did it**. These channels now ship **off**, and the entity turns them on when it decides to — which is the right order, because publishing the correspondence register is part of the entity's publication scheme (Ley 1712 art. 12), not part of installing the software. While off they answer as if they did not exist; once on, **every query and every download is recorded** in the audit trail, with what was asked for and how much was returned. — *Why*: what an entity publishes is its own decision, and it needs to be able to answer afterwards for who consulted its archive — *Impact*: **if your institution already used these channels, they must be explicitly enabled** after updating.

- `fix(gateway,tenant,storage)`: **a crooked URL could reach a different place than the one the system thought it was authorizing (production audit, block 1)** — the gateway decided where to route each request by looking at the path as it arrived, but the library that forwarded it **normalized the path afterwards** — the way `..` in a folder path climbs a level. Result: a hand-crafted URL could be authorized as one thing and executed as another. That path reached the institution registry, deliberately kept out of public scope, and allowed replacing or removing a radicado's attachment **without the trace recording who**. The gateway now **rejects** such paths at the door, before authenticating and before choosing a destination. And that is not the only guard: the institution registry and the attachment-mutating operations additionally require their own service credential, so if one of the two protections failed the other still stands. — *Why*: if the front door authorizes one path and executes another, none of its decisions hold. And not appearing in a route list is not a protection, it is an absence — *Impact*: no visible change in normal use.

- `fix(infra)`: **the production deployment manifest had never been executed (production audit, block 0)** — the file describing how to bring OrpycaMCP up in a real institution was written but never run, and it failed in cascade: seven of the eleven services never built, database initialization pointed at a path that does not exist (and Docker, rather than warning, silently mounts an empty directory), the identity manager died at startup on an incomplete certificate configuration, and none of the secrets the services use to talk to each other were defined — which produced no visible error, just an apparently running system where not a single attachment downloads and nobody with a second factor can sign in. On top of that, any server reboot shut the institution down permanently. All of it is fixed and **verified against real containers**, not merely read: the services build, the database runs its initialization and the identity manager starts. The sample environment file now documents every secret, which two services share it, and **which ones must be backed up with the same discipline as the database** — losing the second-factor key forces the entire staff to re-enrol. — *Why*: with no manifest that starts there is no possible deployment, and no other security check can actually be verified — *Impact*: deployment only.

- `feat(archive)`: **TVD — valuation schedule for an accumulated fonds (ADR-026, Increment 1: registration and convalidation, migration 039)** — an entity holding an accumulated fonds (documentation already produced without archival criteria, typical of suppressed units or liquidated entities) had nowhere to register its valuation instrument, and therefore could neither close nor dispose of anything in that fonds. The TVD enters as **the same instrument as the TRD**, with the same convalidation circuit (Comité → Consejo → RUSD → publication): a discriminator on the existing table, not a new table, so the single point on which seven irreversible WORM retention writes depend is not forked. Four layers protect it: a series code can belong to only one instrument; listings are always read through a view that already filters by type; each instrument's own content (fonds, extreme dates, valuation rationale for the TVD) is mandatory or forbidden depending on the type, enforced by the database; and — most important in this increment — **no expediente can yet be classified under a TVD** (the database blocks it outright), so the irreversible-retention risk is zero until that piece exists. Routes `/api/v1/tvd` mirror the TRD ones (create, edit, version, approve, return, convalidate, register RUSD, repeal), same permission as TRD. Full version-history lookup (`GET .../{code}/versiones`) was also added for both TRD and TVD — promised in the previous increment and never built. — *Why*: a records-compliance audit finding on the retention model: an entity with an accumulated fonds had no way to appraise it inside the system.
- `feat(frontend)`: **`/admin/interoperabilidad` — export/import of radicados, batch loading and postal tracking (Phase 8, last of the API↔UI drift closure)** — five backend capabilities had been ready for a while with no screen using them: exporting a ZIP package of radicados, importing it back preserving the original tracking number, creating documents or expedientes in batch, and querying a shipment's tracking against the postal operator. The new screen covers the first four in tabs; the last one was added as an "Update tracking" button in the existing `/envios` tray. The most delicate part is import: it is all-or-nothing (if a single file in the package does not match its declared fingerprint, nothing is imported) and when that happens the screen shows exactly which file failed and why, never a generic "import failed". Batch loading, being a bulk write over official documents, requires confirming the exact item count before executing, and while processing it polls progress every two seconds, always distinguishing "could not query the status" from "no results yet" (a fabricated zero progress is never shown). Postal tracking, having no real operator integration yet, says so explicitly instead of faking a live query. While building the screen it turned out batch loading could not work at all — the gateway had no route registered for those two endpoints. That was fixed (see *Fixed* below), but the fix changed the shape of the status URL and the screen kept the old one; combined with a reactivity defect in the progress panel, batch loading reported no result under any circumstance. The UX review of 2026-08-02 caught it, and it is **now fixed** in that review's own entry below. Export, import and postal tracking did work end to end from the start. — *Impact*: 1 new screen with 4 sections, 1 new button in postal shipments, 27 new automated tests.
- `feat(frontend)`: **`/admin/tvd` screen + convalidation circuit shared with `/admin/trd` (ADR-026, frontend)** — a structural mirror of the TRD screen with its own columns (fonds/producing unit, extreme dates, instrument state) and no active-archive column, treating the valuation rationale as the form's main field. The convalidation circuit (approve/return/convalidate/register RUSD/repeal) was built once as a **shared** `InstrumentLifecycleActions` component and wired into **both** screens — it did not exist in `/admin/trd` either, ADR-025 had left it pending — warning about irreversibility inside the confirmation modal and offering no button the current state does not allow.
- `fix(archive)`: **remediation of the conformance + security verdict on the TRD convalidation circuit (ADR-025, migration 038)** — the engine now hardens a `convalidada` row (the administrative act is immutable, `estado` may only move to `derogada`, RUSD/publication are fill-once); `devolver` (`aprobada→borrador`, with a mandatory reason) closes the dead end at `aprobada`; `registrar-rusd` records the post-convalidation registry and publication; `derogar` is blocked while `open` expedientes remain under the code; convalidation repeals the previous current version in the same transaction with collision-safe re-pointing of orphaned FKs; `GET /trd/revision-pendiente` gains **no-read-up by clearance plus a query trace** (a security regression: the report did not filter by security level); and the D5 deletion gate authorizes over the frozen snapshot rather than the live row — the original criterion was the opposite of the one the ADR requires and would have blocked legitimate deletions.
- `feat(frontend)`: **PINAR MVP — Institutional Archives Plan in `/admin/pinar` (Phase 7, Acuerdo AGN 003/2015)** — PINAR had 30 endpoints in `tenant-service` and no screen at all. The MVP covers the listing (state filter, creating a new version always in `borrador`) and a workspace with a life-cycle `Stepper` and a dashboard tab (progress per objective / per axis). Approval **permanently freezes the plan's content** (an entry in the immutable `audit_log`), so it is warned about before confirming and the confirm button stays disabled without an administrative act; the dashboard distinguishes "could not load" from a legitimately empty plan, and never paints zeros because of a network failure. Out of scope for the MVP: critical aspects, prioritization, objectives, projects, follow-up, instruments and road map.
- `feat(archive)`: **TRD convalidation circuit — `aprobar`/`convalidar`/`derogar` + ratification of migrated series (ADR-025 Increment 2)** — the state machine that turns the TRD from a configuration table into a convalidated instrument: Comité approval, Consejo convalidation (or ratification of a series backfilled as `migrada`), and repeal, each with its administrative act recorded and audited.
- `feat(frontend)`: **semantic and precedent search in `/busqueda` (Phase 6, E21)** — two new tabs beside the existing exact search, resolved by `?tab=` (deep-linkable), with three SSR proxies including one that verifies the pivot radicado before searching. Non-negotiable interface honesty: results are presented as **similar** documents (vector similarity with an explained qualitative band, never an exact match), a network failure is never disguised as "no results", and knowledge-service's ACL trimming is invisible by design — no hidden-result count is announced, which would turn search into an existence oracle.
- `feat(frontend)`: **generative help (RAG) integrated into drafting and classification (F6, E21)** — RAG is not a separate module: a "Suggest from precedents" button in `/radicar` and `/borradores` calls `POST /knowledge/rag`, and suggested tipo documental chips next to the classification selector are fed by `/knowledge/antecedentes`. Three hard rules: generated text is always inserted marked as generated and with its citations; without citations, insertion is not offered (an unsourced assertion in an official document is worse than no suggestion); and the classification chip is never auto-applied. A knowledge-service failure stays contained in the component and never blocks registering or saving a draft.
- `fix(notification)`: **hardened destination for outbound webhooks** (Medium security finding, non-blocking) — the subscription URL is validated before delivery so the signed webhook cannot be pointed at internal targets.
- `fix(auth)`: **High finding closed — self-escalation to `USUA_PERM_ROOT`; ROOT is now provisioned out of band** ([ADR-024](adr/ADR-024-root-fuera-de-banda.md)) — an entity administrator could grant themselves the ceiling permission of their own entity. ROOT is no longer grantable through the API.
- `fix(tenant,gateway)`: **`app/core/authz.py` + Channel A in tenant-service; `/api/v1/tenants/` removed from the gateway** (closes 2 Critical security-audit findings) — tenant-service had **no authorization at all** (0 gate occurrences against 52 in document-service), and `/api/v1/tenants/` was the only leak **between entities**, closed by taking it out of the gateway. The docstrings claiming "the gateway authorizes" were **deleted**, not just supplemented: that false statement was the vector.
- `fix(document)`: **`USUA_PERM_TRD` + auditing on the metadata catalog (E03, closes a High audit finding)** — the metadata template/element routers wrote with no permission gate.
- `feat(auth)`: **immutable auditing of RBAC/URD/clearance writes (E08, closes a traceability gap)** — granting a permission, changing a membership or raising a clearance now leave an entry in the hash-chained `audit_log`.
- `feat(frontend)`: **TRD/CCD, metadata, catalog and parameter administration (E03/E04/E14, Phase 5 of the API↔UI drift closure)** — TRD/CCD reuses `CatalogCrudPanel` in two tabs, with series as an **indented tree** (the `parent_id` hierarchy is the data, not a flat list) and immutable codes; metadata splits with TRD (TRD = classification, metadata = the fields of those schemas) with create-only templates whose `json_schema` is validated for shape before submitting; catalogs use a master-detail layout that degrades to two navigable levels on mobile; parameters adds a holiday calendar (Ley 51/1983 preload with no external call) and a business-day calculator that reconstructs the **breakdown** of the days subtracted, because the backend only returns the resulting date. **Domain finding reported, not papered over**: document-service's `metadata.py`/`metadata_elements.py` and tenant-service's `catalogos.py`/`config.py` declare **no `require_permission`** — any authenticated tenant user can write. These screens gate their write buttons with `USUA_PERM_ADMIN` client-side as a conservative measure, which never offers *less* than the backend requires.
- `feat(auth)`: **reading group memberships, permissions and clearance (E08, closes the RBAC read gap)** — the backend previously only supported blind add/remove on those three collections.
- `feat(frontend)`: **group and dependencia administration (E08/E14, Phase 5 of the API↔UI drift closure)** — groups reuse `CatalogCrudPanel` with a members/permissions/clearance drawer that **states explicitly** what the backend does not expose; dependencias add a `DependenciaTreeNode.svelte` tree over `GET /dependencias/tree` with an explicit risk-confirmation modal (not a tooltip) when renaming or deactivating, because dependencias are correlated **by name** in `workflow_rules.assign_to_dept` and in in-flight `flow_steps`.
- `feat(frontend)`: **index of classified and reserved information (E08, Ley 1712 art. 20) + final disposition of the radicado/expediente (RF-MET-08/E04, Phase 4 of the API↔UI drift closure)**.
- `feat(frontend)`: **transfer detail with the acta as a certificate and personal signing by role + digital preservation administration (E12/E17/E10, Phase 4 of the API↔UI drift closure)** — each signer's role is derived from their identity, never chosen in the UI.
- `fix(workflow)`: **`workflow_rules.assign_to_dept` validated when the rule is configured + an `audit_log` trace of the silent degradation** — a rule pointing at a non-existent dependencia used to fail quietly at routing time.
- `fix(document)`: **auditing of the radicado annulment cycle (ADR-008/010)**.
- `fix(workflow)`: **forensic auditing of returns, process transactions and cascade reassignment (ADR-008/010)**.
- `feat(workflow)`: **mandatory reason when reverting an assignment (RF-FLU-08)**.
- `feat(frontend)`: **OrpycaMCP Design System v1.0 (E22, ADR-020)** — `_tokens.scss` repainted to the definitive palette keeping every `--op-*` name (zero breakage across 19 screens), with **calculated, not estimated, WCAG contrast**: the primary is split by role (`--op-primary` for non-textual use at 4.28:1, `--op-primary-dark` for text at 6.56:1). v1.0 type scale, radii, shadows and motion + a global `prefers-reduced-motion` rule; **self-hosted Space Grotesk + Public Sans** (`@fontsource`, no CDN). Audited by `svelte-ux-reviewer`: **27 High/Critical findings remediated**, including explicit confirmation before signing in `/firmas`, which had none.
- `fix(frontend)`: **Font Awesome was never loaded and the favicon 404'd (E22)** — its CSS was never imported, so every icon rendered as an empty box.
- `feat(frontend)`: **public landing page at `/` (E22)** — seven sections, with a 302 to `/dashboard` when a session exists; the site root previously did not exist.
- `feat(frontend)`: **login, callback and `/403` with the real brand (E22)** — a split composition and Keycloak error messages mapped from a closed table, which **broke the IdP↔`/login` redirect loop**.
- `feat(frontend)`: **global search in the top bar and configurable favorites in the sidebar (E22)** — the search term travels in the URL (shortcut <kbd>/</kbd>, deep link `?q=`), and the favorites store is namespaced per tenant + user and consumes the already RBAC-filtered list, so a favorite whose permission is withdrawn disappears on its own.
- `feat(frontend)`: **dashboard redesigned around the four personal modules (E22)** — tenant metrics move to a collapsible section gated by `SGD_PERM_ESTADISTICA` (UI mirroring the RBAC), and `NAV_ITEMS` is extracted to `lib/utils/navItems.js` as the single source.
- `feat(frontend)`: **`DataTable` gains the design system's eight mandatory capabilities (E22)** — filters, `aria-sort` ordering, CSV export with formula-injection protection, configurable columns, saved views, multiple selection, `content-visibility` virtualization and in-cell editing, all opt-in; `/busqueda` and the four `/reportes` panels migrated.
- `feat(archive)`: **multi-level EAD 2002 / ISAD(G) archival description of expedientes in OAI-PMH (E11, RF-INT-06)** — a new public endpoint `GET /api/v1/public/archive/oai/{tenant}` disseminates each **public** expediente as a multi-level EAD fragment, walking the `trd_series.parent_id` chain to build fonds→series→(subseries)→file, satisfying ISAD(G) 2.2 and 2.4. **Anti-leak boundary**: it stops at `level="file"` and **never enumerates child radicados' tracking numbers** (a public expediente may contain reserved radicados whose level archive does not know) — only an aggregate count, with a grep guard test. A depth guard was added to the recursive CCD CTE, since the endpoint is anonymous.
- `feat(document)`: **EAD 2002 / ISAD(G) archival description in OAI-PMH (E11, RF-INT-06)** — a second `metadataPrefix=ead` on the same OAI endpoint, disseminating each public radicado as a self-contained EAD fragment with `<archdesc level="item">` and the six mandatory ISAD(G)/NTC 4095 interchange elements. Read-only, no migration: it reuses the `_PUBLIC` chokepoint, so **the format is orthogonal to the security trimming** — choosing `ead` neither bypasses the filter nor creates an oracle.
- `feat(document)`: **interoperable import of radicados (E11, RF-INT-05)** — `POST /api/v1/import` takes the INT-01 ZIP as a raw body with **all-or-nothing validation before touching the DB**: each radicado validates against the service's **own trusted** JSON Schema (not the one shipped in the package, which could be lax) and every binary's SHA-256 is verified; a mismatch returns `422` **with no partial state**. It re-ingests preserving the original radicado number (a collision is skipped, never overwritten), rebuilds the E↔S relation by number, audits `radicado.import` inside the transaction, and guards against zip-slip / zip-bomb. Closes E11's Critical finding.
- `feat(document)`: **interoperable export of radicados (E11, RF-INT-01)** — `POST /api/v1/export` produces a ZIP with a manifest, a **published JSON Schema** the records are validated against, the attachment binaries and per-member SHA-256 checksums, applying **per-radicado no-read-up** and recording over-clearance omissions content-free. The radicado number is preserved as a portable identity, which is what makes INT-05 possible. Mass egress is audited (`radicado.export`).
- `feat(document)`: **minimal read-only CMIS 1.1 profile (E11, RF-INT-02)** — `GET /api/v1/public/cmis/{tenant}` (Browser Binding JSON), where `getContentStream` serves an attachment's bytes only when the radicado is public. Same `_PUBLIC` chokepoint as OAI, so a reserved id yields an indistinguishable 404.
- `feat(document)`: **OAI-PMH — metadata harvesting (E11, RF-INT-02; SGDEA R.12.1)** — the six verbs, Dublin Core, selective harvesting and `resumptionToken` at `GET /api/v1/public/oai/{tenant}`. **Harvesting exposes only what is public** (Ley 1712/2014 arts. 18–19): the trimming lives at the source in a single `_PUBLIC` filter, so the omission is indistinguishable by construction — `completeListSize` counts public records only and a reserved id returns `idDoesNotExist`.
- `feat(tenant)`: **PINAR — Institutional Archives Plan (RF-ADM-08, E14; Acuerdo AGN 003/2015)** — the AGN's six-step methodology as 9 `pinar_*` tables (tenant migration `007`) under `/api/v1/pinar`: diagnosis → critical aspects with risk, five articulating axes, deterministic prioritization, vision, objectives, projects and append-only follow-up (DB trigger). State machine `borrador→aprobado→en_ejecucion→cerrado` with a single plan in execution per tenant, approval requiring an attributable administrative act, and **content frozen on approval** — all audited in the immutable `audit_log` via `orpycamcp_common`, the first use of the shared library in tenant-service.
- `fix(document)` + `feat(document,archive)`: **declared debts closed (E08/E10/E02)** — no-write-up when registering a classified radicado, `SYSTEM_RECONCILER_ID` attribution for reconciliation entries, and subject/type in the expediente export manifest via a clearance-aware internal endpoint.
- `feat(archive)` + `feat(storage)`: **expediente export to ZIP — `GET /api/v1/expedientes/{id}/export.zip` (E02/export; Ley 594/2000 art. 19)** — archive orchestrates and storage assembles via an internal D-02 endpoint: electronic index XML (best-effort) + `manifiesto.csv` including exclusions with their cause + a `LEEME.txt` scope note + `checksums.txt` + the attachment bytes. **Per-radicado no-read-up**: document-service filters `nivel <= clearance` and omits over-clearance radicados including their existence — closing a read-up leak that an expediente-level gate did not cover.
- `feat(document)`: **index of classified and reserved information — `GET /api/v1/reports/indice-reservado(.csv)` (E08; Ley 1712/2014 art. 20)** — the legally mandated register, **content-free** (never the subject) and **orthogonal to clearance** (the register must be complete), gated by `PERM_RECLASIFICAR`, with CSV formula-injection protection. Three conformance blockers were closed, chief among them that the **legal grounds are now mandatory when classifying** (whether registering or reclassifying, 422 if absent), so the index is never left with NULL grounds.
- `feat(document)`: **postal operator as a webhook consumer — inbound callback `POST /api/v1/public/postal/callback/{tenant_slug}` (E20 F5; RF-POR-08; ADR-018)** — per-tenant/per-operator HMAC-SHA256, symmetric to the outbound webhook and separate from the human dispatch permission; the tenant in the path fixes `search_path` and the secret lives in the tenant schema, so there is no cross-tenant forgery. Fail-closed (a missing credential and a bad signature return the same non-oracle 401), idempotent via `UNIQUE (operador, event_id)`, with an atomic transition + audit entry; a late or backwards notification returns `recorded_no_change` instead of pretending.
- `feat(knowledge)`: **generative RAG with citations — `POST /knowledge/rag` (E21 Inc. 3; ADR-006)** — a pluggable `LLMProvider` (`ollama_local` as the sovereign default; external providers and `anthropic` with the native Citations API as options; `disabled` falls back to the citation-only pattern). **Hard sovereignty barrier for generation**: `is_generatable` excludes chunks at `nivel_seguridad >= RESERVADA` from the context when the provider is external — fail-closed, with **no override**, unlike the ADR's original soft model. Honest degradation with `503 rag_unavailable`.
- `feat(knowledge)`: **grounded retrieval with citations — `POST /knowledge/antecedentes` (E21 Inc. 2)** — reuses `search` and its ACL pre-filter, with an anti-oracle 404 collapse on the pivot radicado (it does not distinguish "does not exist" from "exceeds your clearance").
- `feat(knowledge)`: **real, local/sovereign embedding provider (E21 Inc. 1; ADR-006)** — a pluggable `EmbeddingProvider` (`local_st` multilingual sentence-transformers as the deployment default, a deterministic `stub` forced in tests) replaces the stub as the only provider; `EMBEDDING_DIM` (384) is the single source of the vector dimension with a fail-closed startup guard, migration `007` and a `python -m app.ops.reembed` backfill job. With a local provider, classified material **is** embedded — it never leaves the installation — unlike with an external one.
- `feat(storage)`: **PDF/A validation on attachment capture (E07, E10 C2b follow-up; RF-PRE-02)**.
- `feat(storage)` + `feat(archive)`: **index-only AIP for 100% physical expedientes (E10 AIP wiring follow-up; OAIS ISO 14721; RFC 8493)** — an expediente with no digital attachments records `estado='omitido'`: its already-WORM signed index is its artefact.
- `feat(archive)` + `feat(storage)`: **PDF/A profile configurable per documentary series / TRD (E10 C2b follow-up; RF-PRE-02)** — `pdfa_profile` is the explicit exception to the TRD's append-only trigger.
- `feat(archive)` + `feat(storage)`: **WORM renewal of the AIP for Conservación Total series (E10; closes the High debt from the AIP wiring)** — the reconciliation phase now covers all three artefacts (index, acta, AIP), so a CT's complete OAIS package no longer loses its immutability when the original lock expires.
- `feat(archive)` + `feat(document)` + `feat(storage)`: **OAIS AIP wired to expediente closure (E10 debt; OAIS ISO 14721; PREMIS v3; Ac. AGN 001/2024 art. 4.3)** — the endpoint existed but nothing triggered it. Because the AIP must be the **complete** package, attachment `file_id`s are resolved through a new internal document-service endpoint with **no clearance gate** (a system inventory act, unreachable through the gateway; no-read-up is enforced at the AIP's read boundary, not at packaging time). Preconditions: closed + signed index + WORM-protected; a mutated attachment aborts with a fixity 409 rather than producing a corrupt AIP; a session advisory lock closes the irreversible double-packaging race, verified against real concurrent PostgreSQL.
- `feat(storage)`: **real veraPDF PDF/A validation via a hardened subprocess (E10 Increment C2b; RF-PRE-02)** — `create_subprocess_exec` with a fixed argument list and flavour whitelist, **scrubbed ENV** (the child never sees `DATABASE_URL`/`MINIO_SECRET_KEY`/tokens), `start_new_session`+`killpg`+reap guaranteed in `finally` including cancellation, a concurrency semaphore with JVM memory caps, a per-invocation 0700 tempdir, dual output caps against DoS, JSON-only (no XXE), and honest degradation to a `not_evaluated` stub when the binary is absent. The JRE + veraPDF ship only in the deploy image; CI exercises the real plumbing with a fake binary. Tested against real veraPDF 1.30.2.
- `feat(storage)` + `feat(archive)`: **WORM retention renewal for Conservación Total series (E10 debt; Ley 594/2000 + Ac. AGN 001/2024 art. 4.3.2.6)** — a COMPLIANCE object of a permanently conserved series became deletable again once its original `retain_until` elapsed. A new internal `renovar-retencion` endpoint requires a strictly greater extension (otherwise an idempotent no-op) and uses `set_object_retention`; archive gains a reconciliation phase with a local mirror of the retention date, a **fail-closed CT filter in SQL**, a rolling window and exponential backoff with an `agotado` alarm. Only `CT`/`conserve` dispositions are renewed — E/S/M expire and are legitimately disposed of. The irreversible public route was withdrawn (internal only) and a visibility endpoint surfaces CT artefacts at risk.
- `fix(workflow)`: `GET /workflows/{id}/events` scoped by clearance (E05 §10 / RF-SEG-08) — the radicado's route map is only visible to those who can read it (no read-up); operational Channel B, not forensic audit
- `feat(archive)`: expediente route map (E02 §9) — append-only `expediente_eventos` table (migration `archive tenant/014`), events on open/link/unlink/close/transfer, and `GET /api/v1/expedientes/{id}/eventos` scoped by clearance
- `feat(audit)`: `public.audit_log` coverage (E08 §9) — workflow and archive also emit to the cross-cutting forensic audit via `orpycamcp_common.audit` (archive-service now installs the shared library)
- `fix(security)`: full read shielding — detail GETs and semantic search (RF-SEG-08 / RF-BUS-04) — *Why*: close the read surfaces that still didn't apply clearance (direct access by id/tracking and semantic retrieval) — *Impact*: `GET /api/v1/documents/{id}` and `/by-tracking/{tn}` (document-service) and `GET /api/v1/expedientes/{id}` (archive-service) scope the query with `nivel_seguridad <= clearance`; an object above the caller's clearance returns **404** (does not reveal its existence, consistent with search hiding it). Internal/system callers (respuesta, anulación, transfers, index) use `max_clearance=3` by default (no restriction). **knowledge-service**: semantic search no longer trusts the client-supplied `acl` and now **resolves clearance server-side** (`COALESCE(acl->>'nivel_seguridad',1) <= clearance` always; client `acl` only additional narrowing); new `knowledge-service/app/core/clearance.py`. `caller_clearance` helper in archive (resolved after setting `search_path`); `resolve_caller_clearance` made robust with `.get()` for the detail path's double fetch. 71 document + 62 archive + 6 knowledge tests green (4 new). **Read access control closed on ALL surfaces**: search, listing, detail (radicado and expediente) and semantic retrieval
- `fix(document)`: classification-based access control in search (RF-SEG-08 / RF-BUS-04) — *Why*: search only isolated by tenant (`search_path`); within a tenant any user saw every radicado regardless of their clearance — *Impact*: new `radicados.nivel_seguridad` column (migration `document tenant/016`, scale 1=PUBLICA/2=RESERVADA/3=CLASIFICADA = `security_levels.code`, **DEFAULT 1** → no behavior break); `GET /api/v1/search` resolves the caller's clearance from the tenant schema (`auth_users`/`auth_memberships`/`role_clearance`, ADR-013: the DB authorizes) and filters `nivel_seguridad <= clearance` (no read-up, least privilege, *fail-closed* to PUBLICA); `POST /api/v1/documents` accepts `nivel_seguridad` (default 1) to classify; reusable `app/core/clearance.py`; 70 document tests green (7 new: helper + filter), migration validated on real pg
- `fix(archive)`: same clearance scoping on the radicado listing and expediente search (RF-SEG-08 / RF-BUS-04) — *Why*: finish read access control on the surfaces still exposing everything within the tenant — *Impact*: `GET /api/v1/documents` (document-service) now filters the listing by clearance (`find_all`/`service.list` take `max_clearance`); `GET /api/v1/expedientes/search` (archive-service) filters by `nivel_seguridad <= clearance` with the new `expedientes.nivel_seguridad` column (migration `archive tenant/013`, DEFAULT 1) and helper `archive-service/app/core/clearance.py` (resolved after setting the tenant `search_path`); `POST /api/v1/expedientes` accepts `nivel_seguridad` (default 1); 61 archive tests green (6 new), migration validated on real pg. **Read access control closed** across radicado search+listing and expediente search
- `docs(adr)`: ADR-020 — Orpyca design system + frontend conversational assistant (E22) — *Why*: lock down the cross-cutting design/UX and AI-integration decisions before coding the frontend, in a gradual and controlled way — *Impact*: new `docs/es/adr/ADR-020-sistema-diseno-orpyca-asistente.md` (status **Proposed**); decides a single layout + Orpyca tokens as Sass variables **and** `--op-*` custom properties (per-tenant runtime rebranding), role-based UI mirroring the RBAC, and a text/voice assistant that **translates** natural language into MCP tools (ADR-019) — without executing actions itself — with a local LLM (Ollama) and Whisper STT, both *pluggable* and opt-in via Compose profiles; registered in the ADR index (ES+EN)
- `chore(quality)`: pre-commit cleanup before the first source publication — *Why*: to leave the tree clean and compliant with `CONTRIBUTING.md` before the first commit — *Impact*: `ruff check` now passes **clean** (fixed 36 findings: 18 unused imports, 10 unsorted import blocks, `isinstance`/annotations to `X | Y` syntax, `zip(strict=)`, f-string without placeholder); added the missing `.env.example` files (`knowledge-service`, `signature-service`); obsolete scratch notes (`config.txt`) moved out of the versioned tree; `compileall` OK and `shared` tests green after the autofixes
- `chore(license)`: attribution/license header across the whole source tree — *Why*: to record authorship and license terms in every file — *Impact*: added to **437 files** (361 `.py`, 63 `.sql`, 11 Dockerfile, 2 `.js`) a header «Desarrollo original de Orpyca; adaptado a partir de Orfeo bajo licencia GPL – aurigadl@gmail.com» with the AGPL v3 notice (the project's license, derived from the original Orfeo's GPL); applied using each type's comment syntax; verified: `compileall` OK and **347 tests green** across the 11 services after the change
- `feat(mcp-server)`: per-session OAuth — the MCP obtains/refreshes the Keycloak token (E18, ADR-019) — *Why*: to complete E18 without relying on a static bearer: the mcp-server manages the token lifecycle — *Impact*: `app/services/oauth.py` (`TokenManager`) obtains/refreshes the access token via `auth-service` (`/api/v1/auth/token` ROPC and `/api/v1/auth/refresh`, public in the gateway) with expiry caching; precedence `MCP_BEARER_TOKEN` (static) → `MCP_REFRESH_TOKEN` → `MCP_AUTH_USERNAME`/`MCP_AUTH_PASSWORD`; over HTTP the bearer still arrives via header; 27 tests green (4 new). **E18 (MCP layer) complete**: tools + resources + prompts + stdio + HTTP streamable + per-session OAuth
- `feat(mcp-server)`: MCP resources and prompts (E18, ADR-019) — *Why*: to complete the three MCP capabilities (tools + resources + prompts) so the agent can read data by URI and use reusable templates — *Impact*: `app/mcp_resources.py` registers **resources** (`orfeo://normativa` static + templates `orfeo://radicado/{id}`, `orfeo://expediente/{id}`, `orfeo://expediente/{id}/indice` resolved against the gateway via the catalog) and **prompts** (`radicar_pqrsd`, `resumen_expediente`, `buscar_antecedentes`); available on both transports; the server advertises `tools`/`resources`/`prompts` capabilities (verified over the real protocol); 23 tests green (7 new). Pending: only per-session OAuth
- `feat(mcp-server)`: MCP HTTP streamable transport for remote clients (E18, ADR-019) — *Why*: to complete the MCP binding with the remote transport in addition to stdio — *Impact*: **`POST /mcp`** endpoint mounted on the FastAPI app with `StreamableHTTPSessionManager` (stateless + JSON); the session context arrives via headers (`Authorization`/`X-Tenant-Slug`/`X-User-Permissions`/`X-User-Roles`) and is injected through a `ContextVar` (stdio still uses the environment); `mcp_app.py` refactored for a unified stdio/HTTP context; **`initialize` handshake over `/mcp` verified** in a test; 16 tests green (2 new). Pending: per-session OAuth + resources/prompts
- `feat(mcp-server)`: working MCP server over stdio (E18, ADR-019) — *Why*: to bind the official MCP SDK over the existing catalog/dispatcher so agents/LLMs can consume OrpycaMCP via the MCP protocol — *Impact*: `app/mcp_app.py` (an `mcp.server.Server` that exposes the catalog as MCP tools with inputSchema, filtered by permissions, and runs them via the dispatcher against the gateway propagating token+tenant) + `app/mcp_stdio.py` (entrypoint `python -m app.mcp_stdio`); `mcp>=1.2.0` dependency; **real stdio handshake verified** (`initialize` protocolVersion 2024-11-05 + `tools/list` → 8 tools); session context via environment (`MCP_BEARER_TOKEN`/`MCP_TENANT_SLUG`/`MCP_USER_PERMISSIONS`/`MCP_USER_ROLES`); 14 tests green (5 new). Pending: HTTP streamable transport + per-session OAuth + resources/prompts
- `docs(ops)`: external-integration operations notes (`integraciones-externas.md`, ES+EN) — *Why*: to guide activating the deferred external components once the environment is available — *Impact*: documents, for each pluggable capability, the swap point, the stable contract and the steps: PKI/XAdES qualified signature (signature-service `signer.py`), WORM/Object-Lock (storage-service), PDF/A validation with veraPDF, MCP SDK binding (mcp-server), real embeddings/RAG (knowledge-service `embeddings.py`), real postal operator (document-service `postal_provider.py`); added to the nav; clean `--strict` build
- `docs(i18n)`: complete publishable documentation in Spanish and English — *Why*: the MkDocs site is bilingual but `docs/en/` only had 3 stale pages; the rest fell back to Spanish — *Impact*: all 14 nav pages translated to English (index, domain, normativa, lifecycle, getting-started, architecture, multi-tenancy, adrs, api, deployment, roadmap, contributing, security-audit, changelog); `deployment.md` added to the nav; fixed broken links to `documentos/specDrive/` in ADR-010/012 and accented anchors in `deployment.md`; **clean `mkdocs build --strict`** (es + en, no warnings). The 19 ADR detail pages still fall back to Spanish (technical decision records)
- `feat(document)`: extended statistical dashboard + CSV export (E09, AGN-mandated) — *Why*: entities periodically report statistics to oversight bodies — *Impact*: `GET /api/v1/reports/radicados` adds `by_dependencia`; new `GET /api/v1/reports/radicados.csv` exports the summary (type/status/month/dependencia sections) as CSV with `Content-Disposition`; filters `?date_from=&date_to=`; 63 document tests green (1 new)
- `feat(document)`: citizen PQRS registration (public, Ley 1755/2015) — *Why*: a legal requirement for public entities; a citizen must be able to file a petition without logging in and then track it — *Impact*: `POST /api/v1/public/{tenant}/pqrs` (unauthenticated, tenant in the path) creates an Entrada radicado with `metadata.canal=pqrs_ciudadano` and type (petición/queja/reclamo/sugerencia/denuncia), reusing the radicación and verification code from E13; returns `{radicado_id, tracking_number, verification_code}` for tracking via `/public/{tenant}/verify/{code}`; already routed as public in the gateway; 62 document tests green (2 new)
- `feat(document)`: Salida templates + drafts (legacy parity) — *Why*: to produce Salida documents (create/edit/approve/register) before assigning them an official number; templates provide reusable content — *Impact*: `plantilla` and `borrador` tables (migration `document tenant/015`); `GET/POST/DELETE /api/v1/plantillas`; `POST/GET /api/v1/borradores` (optional creation from a template), `GET/PATCH /api/v1/borradores/{id}` (edit only if not yet registered), `POST .../aprobar` (draft→approved), `POST .../radicar` (→ generates the real radicado reusing the radicación, marks `radicado` with `radicado_id`); routed in the gateway; 60 document tests green (4 new); migration validated on real pg
- `docs(api)`: document tenant administration (E14) — dependencias, catalogs, parameters, **non-business days/holidays** (`GET/POST/DELETE /api/v1/config/holidays`, `/seed`) and `business-days/calculate` — *Why*: the holidays CRUD already existed (config.py) but was not in `api.md`; the audit had flagged it as a gap, which was a false positive
